(a) he is able to fulfil his need with a given level of
income
(b) he is able to live in full comforts with a given level
of income
(c) he can fulfil his needs without consumption of
certain items
(d) he is able to locate new sources of income
Answer: (a)
• Consumer equilibrium is a fundamental
concept in microeconomics that describes a
state of maximum satisfaction.
• It is the point at which a consumer has
allocated their given level of income among
various goods and services to achieve the
highest possible utility.
• This state is determined by the consumer's
budget and the current prices of goods.
• The goal is to get the most value and fulfill
needs within financial constraints, not
necessarily to live in “full comforts” or find
new income sources.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q7. The supply-side economics lays greater emphasis
on the point of view of
(a) producer
(b) global economy

(c) consumer
(d) middle-man
Answer: (a)
• Supply-side economics is a macroeconomic
theory that argues that economic growth can
be most effectively created by lowering
barriers for people to produce (supply) goods
and services.
• The focus is on the producer (the “supply”
side) rather than the consumer (the
“demand” side).
• Key policy recommendations include lower
tax rates and deregulation to incentivize
production and investment.
• The theory posits that by encouraging
production, consumers will ultimately benefit
from a greater supply of goods and services,
lower prices, and increased employment
opportunities.
• This is in direct contrast to Keynesian
economics, which emphasizes the role of
aggregate demand in driving economic
growth.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2000
Q1. The Standing Committee of State Finance
Ministers recommended in January 2000 uniform
rates across the States in respect of
(a) value-added tax
(b) sales tax
(c) stamp duty and registration fees
(d) agricultural income-tax
Answer: (b) sales tax
Broader Explanation:
• The Standing Committee of State Finance
Ministers in January 2000 recommended
uniform rates for sales tax across all states.
• This was a step towards a more unified tax
structure in India, which eventually led to the
implementation of the Goods and Services
Tax (GST).

•

The goal was to reduce tax competition
among states and create a more seamless
national market.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. Indian Human Development Report does not
give for each sample village
(a) Infrastructure and Amenities Index
(b) Education Related Index
(c) Health Related Index
(d) Unemployment Related Index
Answer: (d) Unemployment Related Index
Broader Explanation:
• The Human Development Index (HDI) is a
composite statistic used to rank countries
into four tiers of human development.
• It is based on three key dimensions: life
expectancy, education, and per capita
income.
• While the Indian Human Development
Report provides indices for infrastructure,
education, and health, it does not typically
include a specific Unemployment Related
Index for each sample village.
• Unemployment data is often collected at a
broader (national or state) level rather than
at the village level for this report.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. The Swarna Jayanti Shahari Rozgar Yojana which
came into operation from Dec. 1, 1997 aims to
provide gainful employment to the urban
unemployed or underemployed poor but does not
include
(a) Nehru Rozgar Yojana
(b) Urban Basic Services Programme
(c) Prime Minister’s Integrated Urban Poverty
Eradication Programme
(d) Prime Minister’s Rozgar Yojana
Answer: (c)
• The Swarna Jayanti Shahari Rozgar Yojana
(SJSRY) was a centrally sponsored scheme
launched on December 1, 1997, to address
urban poverty.

•

It aimed to provide gainful employment to
the urban unemployed and underemployed
poor through self-employment ventures and
wage employment.
• The SJSRY was a merger of three earlier
schemes: Nehru Rozgar Yojana (NRY), Urban
Basic Services for the Poor (UBSP), and the
Prime Minister’s Integrated Urban Poverty
Eradication Programme (PMIUPEP) was not a
part of it.
• The Prime Minister's Rozgar Yojana (PMRY)
was a separate scheme and was not merged
into SJSRY.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4. Gilt-edged market means
(a) bullion market
(b) market of Government securities
(c) market of guns
(d) market of pure metals
Answer: (b)
• The gilt-edged market is the market for
government securities.
• These securities are considered risk-free
because they are issued or guaranteed by the
government, which is highly unlikely to
default on its payment obligations.
• The term "gilt-edged" means "of the best
quality", reflecting the high creditworthiness
of these securities.
• This market plays a crucial role in the
country's financial system and is used by the
government to raise funds.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. Consider the following statements:
The Indian rupee is fully convertible
1. in respect of Current Account of Balance of
Payment.
2. in respect of Capital Account of Balance of
Payment.
3. into gold.
Which of these statements is/are correct?
(a) 1 alone
(b) 3 alone

(c) 1 and 2
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (a)
• Currency convertibility refers to the freedom
to convert a country's currency into foreign
currencies and vice versa.
• The Indian rupee is currently fully convertible
on the current account, meaning it can be
freely exchanged for transactions related to
trade in goods and services, and remittances.
• However, there are still restrictions on the
capital account, which deals with investments
and borrowings. This is known as partial
capital account convertibility.
• The rupee is not convertible into gold at a
fixed rate; its value is determined by the
foreign exchange market.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q6. Resurgent India Bonds were issued in US Dollar,
Pound Sterling and
(a) Japanese Yen
(b) Deutsche Mark
(c) Euro
(d) French Franc
Answer: (b)
• Resurgent India Bonds (RIBs) were issued by
the State Bank of India in 1998 to raise
foreign currency deposits from Non-Resident
Indians (NRIs).
• The bonds were denominated in three
currencies: US Dollar, Pound Sterling, and
Deutsche Mark.
• The Deutsche Mark was the currency of
Germany at the time, before the introduction
of the Euro.
• The funds raised through these bonds were
used to shore up India's foreign exchange
reserves.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7. Match List I (Power generation plant) with List II

List II

ABCD

• This question matches industrial plants with

Q8. Consider the following statements:
The Ministerial Meeting of the WTO held in
December 1999 was unsuccessful because it
attempted to link trade with
1. labour-related issues.
2. environment related issues.
3. terrorism related issues.
4. debt-related issues.
Which of these statements are correct?
(a) 1, 3 and 4
(b) 1 and 2
(c) 2 and 3
(d) 2 and 4

Answer: (b)
• The 1999 WTO Ministerial Conference in
Seattle was met with massive protests,
famously known as the "Battle in Seattle".
• The negotiations failed largely because of
disagreements over the inclusion of labour
and environmental standards in trade
agreements.
• Developing countries were concerned that
such standards would be used as a form of
protectionism by developed countries.
• The protests highlighted the growing public
concern about the social and environmental
impacts of globalization.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q10. The growth rate of per capita income at current
prices is higher than that of per capita income at
constant prices, because the latter takes into account
the rate of
(a) growth of population
(b) increase in price level
(c) growth of money supply
(d) increase in the wage rate
Answer: (b)
• Per capita income at current prices is
calculated using the prices of the current
year, so it includes the effect of inflation.
• Per capita income at constant prices (real per
capita income) is adjusted for inflation by
using the prices of a base year.
• Therefore, the growth rate of per capita
income at current prices will be higher than
at constant prices because it includes the
increase in the price level (inflation).
• The difference between the two growth rates
is a measure of the rate of inflation.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q11. The first Indian State to have its Human
Development Report prepared and released by
Amartya Kumar Sen in Delhi is
(a) West Bengal
(b) Kerala

(c) Madhya Pradesh
(d) Andhra Pradesh
Answer: (c)
• Madhya Pradesh was the first state in India
to prepare and release its own Human
Development Report (HDR).
• The report was released in 1995 by Nobel
laureate Amartya Kumar Sen.
• This was a significant step in decentralizing
the human development approach and
focusing on sub-national disparities.
• The report analyzed various development
indicators at the district level, providing
valuable insights for policymaking.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q12. A rise in ‘SENSEX’ means
(a) a rise in prices of shares of all companies
registered with Bombay Stock Exchange
(b) a rise in prices of shares of all companies
registered with National Stock Exchange
(c) an overall rise in prices of shares of group of
companies registered with Bombay Stock Exchange
(d) a rise in prices of shares of all companies belonging
to a group of companies registered with Bombay
Stock Exchange.
Answer: (c)
• The SENSEX, or the BSE Sensitive Index, is a
stock market index of the Bombay Stock
Exchange (BSE).
• It is a weighted average of the prices of 30 of
the largest and most actively traded stocks
on the BSE.
• A rise in the SENSEX indicates an overall
increase in the market value of these 30
companies, which is often seen as a
barometer of the health of the Indian
economy.
• It does not mean that the prices of all shares
on the BSE have risen, but rather that the
prices of the shares in the SENSEX basket
have risen on average.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 2001
Q1. Which of the following committees examined
and suggested Financial Sector Reforms?
(a) Abid Hussain Committee
(b) Bhagwati Committee
(c) Chelliah Committee
(d) Narasimham Committee
Answer: (d)
• The Narasimham Committee was established
under former RBI Governor M. Narasimham
in August 1991 to examine all aspects of the
financial system in India.
• The committee provided comprehensive
recommendations for financial sector
reforms, including the banking sector and
capital markets.
• Key recommendations included reduction in
SLR and CRR, redefining the priority sector,
and deregulation of interest rates.
• It also addressed Asset Classification, defined
Non-Performing Assets (NPAs), and
recommended setting up tribunals for loan
recovery.
• The committee's recommendations formed
the foundation of India's banking reforms in
the 1990s.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. The new series of Wholesale Price Index (WPI)
released by the Government of India is with
reference to the base prices of
(a) 1981-82
(b) 1990-91
(c) 1993-94
(d) 1994-95
Answer: (c) 1993-94
Broader Explanation:
• The Wholesale Price Index (WPI) represents
the price of goods at the wholesale stage,
i.e., goods sold in bulk and traded between
organizations.
• At the time this question was asked, the base
year for WPI was 1993-94. (Currently, it has
been updated to 2011-12.)

•

WPI is used as a measure of inflation, though
India now primarily uses the Consumer Price
Index (CPI) for this purpose.
• The base year is periodically updated to
reflect changes in the economy and
consumption patterns.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. The term National Income represents
(a) Gross National Product at market prices minus
depreciation
(b) Gross National Product at market prices minus
depreciation net factor income from abroad
(c) Gross National Product at market prices minus
depreciation and indirect taxes subsidies
(d) Gross National Product at market prices minus net
factor income from abroad
Answer: (c)
• National Income measures the total value of
all goods and services produced in a country
during a specific period.
• It is calculated as Gross National Product
(GNP) at market prices minus depreciation
(wear and tear of capital assets) minus
indirect taxes plus subsidies.
• This gives us Net National Product (NNP) at
factor cost, which is also known as National
Income.
• GNP includes income earned by the country's
citizens abroad minus the income earned by
foreigners within the country.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4. Assertion (A): There was an increase in industrial
production during 1999-2000.
Reason (R): The period witnessed a stable exchange
rate and improved business sentiments.
(a) Both A and R are individually true, and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true, but R is NOT a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true, but R is false
(d) A is false, but R is true
Answer: (a) During 1999-2000, India experienced a
rise in industrial production.

•

This growth was supported by a stable
exchange rate, reducing uncertainty for
businesses involved in international trade.
• Improved business sentiments encouraged
investment and production.
• A stable macroeconomic environment helps
businesses expand operations and invest in
new projects, explaining the rise in industrial
output.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. Assertion (A): India's software exports increased
at an average growth rate of 50% since 1995-96.
Reason (R): Indian software companies were cost
effective and maintained international quality.
(a) Both A and R are individually true, and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true, but R is NOT a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true, but R is false
(d) A is false, but R is true
Answer: (a)
• India's software exports grew at around 50%
annually since 1995-96.
• This was due to cost-effectiveness and
international quality standards maintained
by Indian companies.
• A large pool of skilled IT professionals and
supportive government policies further
boosted growth.
• These factors directly explain the rapid
growth in exports.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q6. Assertion (A): Ceiling on foreign exchange for a
host of current account transaction heads was
lowered in the year 2000.
Reason (R): There was a fall in foreign currency
assets also.
(a) Both A and R are individually true, and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true, but R is NOT a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true, but R is false
(d) A is false, but R is true

Answer: (b)
• In 2000, the ceiling on foreign exchange for
various current account transactions was
lowered.
• There was also a fall in foreign currency
assets during this period.
• However, the fall in foreign currency assets
was not the reason for lowering ceilings.
• The two events occurred simultaneously but
were not causally linked.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7. The prices at which the Government purchases
food grains for maintaining the public distribution
system and for building up buffer-stocks is known as
(a) minimum support prices
(b) procurement prices
(c) issue prices
(d) ceiling prices
Answer: (b) procurement prices
Broader Explanation:
• Procurement prices are the prices at which
the government purchases commodities from
producers to maintain buffer stocks or the
public distribution system (PDS).
• These are announced by the Government of
India, usually on the recommendation of the
Commission for Agricultural Costs and Prices
(CACP).
• MSP is the guaranteed price, while
procurement price is the actual purchase
price for government stocks.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q10. Consider the following:
1. Market borrowing
2. Treasury bills
3. Special securities issued to RBI
Which of these is/are component(s) of internal debt?
(a) I only
(b) I and II
(c) II only
(d) I, II and III
Answer: (d) Internal debt is the part of total debt
owed to lenders within the country.

•

It includes:
o Market borrowings through
government bonds.
o Treasury Bills (T-Bills), which are
short-term government debt
instruments.
o Special securities issued to the RBI,
representing borrowing from the
central bank.
• Internal debt is preferred over external debt
because it does not create foreign exchange
obligations.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q11. Consider the following statements regarding
Reserve Bank of India:
I. It is a banker to the Central Government.
II. It formulates and administers monetary policy.
III. It acts as an agent of the Government in respect of
India's membership of IMF.
IV. It handles the borrowing programme of
Government of India.
Which of these statements are correct?
(a) I and II
(b) II, III and IV
(c) I, II, III and IV
(d) III and IV
• Statement I is correct: RBI acts as banker to
the Central Government, managing money,
remittance, exchange, and transactions.
• Statement II is correct: RBI formulates and
administers monetary policy to ensure
economic stability.
• Statement III is incorrect: While RBI manages
foreign exchange, it does not act as India's
IMF agent in the sense implied.
• Statement IV is partially correct but not fully
accurate, as RBI manages government debt
but does not solely handle the borrowing
programme.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q12. Consider the following schemes launched by
the Union Government:
1. Antyodaya Anna

2. Gram Sadak Yojana
3. Sarvapriya
4. Jawahar Gram Samriddhi Yojana
Which of these were announced in the year 2000?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 4
(c) 3 and 4
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (a)
• Antyodaya Anna Yojana (AAY) was launched
on 25 December 2000 to provide highly
subsidized food to the poorest families.
• Pradhan Mantri Gram Sadak Yojana (PMGSY)
was launched in 2000 to provide all-weather
road connectivity to unconnected villages.
• Sarvapriya Scheme was launched in 2001, not
2000.
• Jawahar Gram Samriddhi Yojana (JGSY) was
launched on 1 April 1999, not 2000.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q13. The most appropriate measure of a country's
economic growth is its
(a) Gross Domestic Product
(b) Net Domestic Product
(c) Net National Product
(d) Per Capita Real Income
Answer: (d)
• GDP, NDP, and NNP measure total economic
output, but they don’t account for
population size.
• Per Capita Real Income divides national
income by population and adjusts for
inflation, reflecting average standard of
living.
• Even if total income grows, rapid population
growth can offset its effect on living
standards.
• Hence, it is a better indicator of economic
welfare.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q14. Consider the following factors regarding an
industry:
1. Capital investments

2. Business turnover
3. Labour force
4. Power consumption
Which of these determine the nature and size of the
industry?
(a) 1, 3 and 4
(b) 1, 2 and 4
(c) 2, 3 and 4
(d) 2 and 3
Answer: (b)
•

Capital investments (1) indicate the scale of
operations.
• Business turnover (2) reflects the volume of
activity and revenue.
• Power consumption (4) indicates production
capacity and scale.
• Labour force (3) alone is not a reliable
indicator, as modern industries can be
capital-intensive with fewer workers.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q15. Consider the following statements regarding
power sector in India:
I. The installed capacity of power generation is around
95000 MW.
II. Nuclear plants contribute nearly 15% of total power
generation.
III. Hydroelectricity plants contribute nearly 40% of
total power generation.
IV. Thermal plants at present account for nearly 80%
of total power generation.
Which of these statements is/are correct?
(a) I only
(b) II and III
(c) III and IV
(d) I and IV
Answer: (a)
• Statement I was correct at the time: Installed
capacity was around 95,000 MW (in 2001).
• Statement II is incorrect: Nuclear contributed
only 2-3%, not 15%.
• Statement III is incorrect: Hydro contributed
around 16%, not 40%.

•

Statement IV is incorrect: Thermal accounted
for about 65%, not 80%.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q16. National Agriculture Insurance Scheme
replacing Comprehensive Crop Insurance Scheme
was introduced in the year
(a) 1997
(b) 1998
(c) 1999
(d) 2000
Answer: (c)
• The National Agricultural Insurance Scheme
(NAIS) or Rashtriya Krishi Bima Yojana (RKBY)
was introduced in 1999-2000.
• It replaced the Comprehensive Crop
Insurance Scheme, which had failed to meet
objectives.
• NAIS covers food crops, oilseeds,
horticultural and commercial crops.
• Operates on an area approach for widespread
calamities and individual approach for
localized disasters.
• Small and marginal farmers get 50%
premium subsidy, shared by Centre and
States.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2003
Q1.In India, the first bank of limited liability
managed by Indians and founded in 1881 was:
(a) Hindustan Commercial Bank
(b) Oudh Commercial Bank
(c) Punjab National Bank
(d) Punjab and Sind Bank
Ans. (b)
Explanation:
• The Oudh Commercial Bank was established
in 1881 in Faizabad (U.P.), and it was the first
bank with limited liability to be managed
entirely by Indians.
• It was a commercial bank and served local
needs, but it was relatively small in size and
did not have any branches.

•

It represented an early indigenous effort to
establish financial institutions in colonial
India, during a period when most banks were
European-owned and managed.
• The bank continued for several decades but
eventually failed in 1958, mainly due to lack
of capital base and modern banking
practices.
• Its legacy is important as it paved the way for
later Indian-owned banks, including Punjab
National Bank (1894).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2.The government holding in BPCL is:
(a) more than 70%
(b) between 60% and 70%
(c) between 50% and 60%
(d) less than 50%
Ans. (c)
Explanation:
• The Government of India held around 52%
stake in Bharat Petroleum Corporation
Limited (BPCL).
• BPCL is a public sector oil and gas company
headquartered in Mumbai, with major
refineries in Mumbai and Kochi.
• It has been one of India’s key companies in
the petroleum refining and marketing sector.
• The government’s majority shareholding
(>50%) ensured it remained under public
sector control, though disinvestment plans
have been initiated in recent years.
• BPCL has featured on the Fortune Global 500
list, indicating its global economic scale.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3.Which one among the following countries has the
lowest GDP per capita?
(a) China
(b) India
(c) Indonesia
(d) Sri Lanka
Ans. (b)
Explanation:

•

GDP per capita is calculated as GDP ÷
population, indicating average income per
person.
• In the early 2000s, India had a lower per
capita income than China, Indonesia, and Sri
Lanka due to:
o High population base, which dilutes
average per capita figures.
o Lower industrial output and export
earnings compared to China.
o Service sector growth was high but
not evenly distributed across the
population.
• China had already begun its manufacturingled economic surge, while Indonesia and Sri
Lanka had higher per capita incomes due to
smaller populations and steady growth.
• By nominal GDP and PPP (Purchasing Power
Parity) both methods, India lagged behind
these countries during this period.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. During the year 2000-01, which one of the
following industries recorded highest growth rate in
India?
(a) Cement
(b) Coal
(c) Electricity
(d) Steel
Ans. (d)
Explanation:
• In 2000–01, the Steel industry witnessed the
highest growth rate among core industries.
• India is the world’s third-largest producer of
crude steel (up from 8th in 2003), expected to
become second by mid-2010s.
• Growth was driven by:
o Domestic availability of raw
materials, particularly iron ore.
o Cost-effective labour and
modernization of steel mills.
o Increased infrastructure investment,
particularly in roads, housing, and
transport.

o

FDI inflows in metallurgical industries,
with over US$ 8.89 billion between
2000–2016.
• The automobile and railways sectors also
boosted steel consumption, making it the
highest growing core sector during that year.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q5. Consider the following statements:
1. In the last five years, Indian software exports
have increased at a compound annual growth
rate of about 60%
2. The software and service industry in India
registered an overall growth of about 28% in
rupee terms during the year 2001-2002.
Which of these statements is/are correct?
(a) Only 1
(b) Only 2
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Ans. (c)
• The Indian software exports during the late
1990s and early 2000s experienced a
remarkable boom, growing at a CAGR of
about 60%.
• IT services, software exports, and BPOs
became major contributors to India’s foreign
exchange earnings.
• In 2001–02, despite the global slowdown
after the dot-com burst, the sector still
registered around 28% growth in rupee
terms, indicating resilience.
• The success was driven by English-speaking
skilled manpower, cost advantage, Y2Krelated demand, and outsourcing trends
from developed countries.
• This marked India’s emergence as a global IT
outsourcing hub.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q6. Which one of the following statements is NOT
correct?
(a) India is the second largest producer of nitrogenous
fertilizers in the world

(b) India is the ninth largest steel producing country in
the world
(c) India is the second largest producer of silk in the
world
(d) India ranks fourth in the world in coal production.
Ans. (a)
• India is not the second largest producer of
nitrogenous fertilizers, this statement is
incorrect.
• India ranks among the top producers, but
China leads globally in nitrogenous fertilizer
production.
• In steel, India has moved from 9th to 3rd
largest producer globally by 2016.
• India is the second largest silk producer after
China.
• India ranks 4th in coal production globally
after China, USA, and Australia.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q7.With reference to Government of India’s
decisions regarding Foreign Direct Investment (FDI)
during the year 2001-02, consider the following
statements:
1. Out of the 100% FDI allowed by India in a tea
sector, the foreign firm would have to
disinvest 33% of the equity in favour of an
Indian Partner within four years.
2. Regarding the FDI in print media in India, the
single largest Indian shareholder should have
a holding higher than 26%
Which of these statements is/are correct?
(a) Only 1
(b) Only 2
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Ans. (b)
Explanation:
• In 2002, the Union Cabinet allowed 100% FDI
in the tea sector through the FIPB route, but
with a condition of compulsory
disinvestment of 26%, not 33%, in favour of
an Indian partner within 5 years, not 4. So
statement 1 is incorrect.

•

For FDI in print media, the policy required
that the single largest Indian shareholder
must hold more than 26%, ensuring Indian
ownership and editorial control remained
predominant. Statement 2 is correct.
• These FDI policy reforms were part of the
liberalization measures to attract foreign
investment while maintaining national
interest safeguards.
• Print media remained more restricted
compared to other sectors due to sensitivity
over national information control.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q8.Among the following commodities imported by
India during the year 2000-01, which one was the
highest in terms of Rupee value?
(a) Edible oil
(b) Fertilizers
(c) Organic and inorganic chemicals
(d) Pearls, precious and semi-precious stones
Ans. (d)
Explanation:
• Pearls, precious and semi-precious stones
were the highest import item by value in
2000–01.
• India is one of the largest centres for cutting,
polishing, and re-exporting diamonds and
gemstones, especially in Surat and Mumbai.
• Most of these are imported as raw or uncut
stones, value added through cutting and
polishing, and then exported, making India a
global processing hub.
• This category forms a major share of
merchandise imports, even though domestic
consumption is relatively low.
• The sector has a significant contribution to
foreign exchange earnings, employment, and
trade surplus in gems and jewellery.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q9.The Kelkar proposals which were in the news
recently were the:
(a) recommendations for reforms in the power sector.
(b) recommendations for tax reforms.

(c) guidelines for the privatization of public sector
undertakings.
(d) guidelines for reducing vehicular pollution, and the
promotion of CNG use.
Ans. (b)
Explanation:
• The Kelkar Committee (2002), headed by
Vijay Kelkar, was a Task Force on Direct and
Indirect Taxes.
• It made major tax reform recommendations,
including:
o Increase in income tax exemption
limit to ₹1 lakh.
o Rationalization of income tax slabs,
removal of surcharges.
o Abolition of wealth tax and longterm capital gains tax.
o Reduction of corporate tax to 30%
(domestic) and 35% (foreign).
o Abolition of Minimum Alternate Tax
and rationalization of exemptions.
o PAN expansion, faster return
processing, and modernization of tax
administration through Tax
Information Network.
• These proposals aimed to simplify,
modernize, and widen the tax base to
promote compliance and growth.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q10.Consider the following statements:
1. The maximum limit of shareholding of Indian
promoters in private sector banks in India is
49 percent of the paid up capital.
2. Foreign Direct Investment upto 49 percent
from all sources is permitted in private sector
banks in India under the automatic route.
Which of these statements is/are correct?
(a) Only 1
(b) Only 2
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Ans. (b)
Explanation:

•

At that time, Indian promoters could hold
more than 49% in certain cases, hence
statement 1 is not correct.
• FDI up to 49% was permitted in private sector
banks under the automatic route, and
beyond 49% up to 74% was allowed with FIPB
approval, making statement 2 correct.
• This was part of financial sector liberalization
to infuse foreign capital, technology, and
governance into the banking system.
• Portfolio investments (FII + NRI + OCB) were
also capped at 49%.
• These norms ensured Indian ownership and
control while encouraging foreign
participation in banking.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q11.Debenture holders of a company are its:
(a) Shareholders
(b) Creditors
(c) Debtors
(d) Directors
Ans. (b)
Explanation:
• Debentures are long-term debt instruments
issued by companies to borrow funds.
• Debenture holders are creditors, not owners,
of the company — they lend money and in
return receive fixed interest, regardless of
profit.
• They do not have voting rights in company
meetings, unlike shareholders.
• Debenture interest is a legal obligation and
paid before dividends.
• Debentures are tradable in stock exchanges,
often secured, and usually repayable after a
fixed period.
• Debenture holders have a priority claim over
assets compared to shareholders in case of
liquidation.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q12.Consider the following statements:
In India, stamp duties on financial transactions are:
1. levied and collected by the State Government.

2. appropriated by the Union Government.
Which of these statements is/are correct?
(a) Only 1
(b) Only 2
(c) both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Ans. (d)
Explanation:
• Stamp duties on items like bills of exchange,
cheques, promissory notes etc. are levied by
the Central Government, but collected and
appropriated by the States.
• Therefore, neither of the statements as given
is correct:
o Statement 1 is incorrect because
States do not levy these duties.
o Statement 2 is incorrect because the
Union does not appropriate these
proceeds — they go to the States.
• This arrangement is outlined under Article
268 of the Constitution, dealing with taxes
levied by the Union but collected and
appropriated by the States.
• This ensures uniformity of taxation while
supporting State revenues.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2004
Q1. Which one of the following statements is not
correct?
(a) Under the Targeted Public Distribution System, the
families Below Poverty Line are provided 50 kg of food
grains per month per family at subsidised price
(b) Under Annapurna Scheme, indigent senior citizens
of 65 years of age or above eligible for National Old
Age Pension but not getting pension can get 10 kg of
food grains per person per month free of cost
(c) Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment has
scheme in which indigent people living in welfare
institutions like orphanages are given 15 kg of food
grains per person per month at BPL rates
(d) Ministry of Human Resource Development gives
financial support to Mid-day Meal Scheme for the

benefit of class I to V students in Government or
Government-aided Schools
Answer: (a)
• Statement (a) is incorrect. The Targeted
Public Distribution System (TPDS), introduced
in June 1997, did not provide 50 kg of food
grains. Initially, it involved the issue of 10 kg
of food grains per family per month for the
BPL population at specially subsidized prices.
The quantity has been revised over the years,
but it was never 50 kg per family.
• The TPDS focuses on the poor in all areas and
requires states to implement foolproof
arrangements for identification, effective
delivery of food grains to Fair Price Shops
(FPS), and transparent distribution.
• Statement (b) is correct. The Annapurna
Scheme, launched on April 1, 2000, provides
10 kg of food grains per person per month
free of cost to senior citizens (65+) who are
eligible for the National Old Age Pension
Scheme (NOAPS) but are not receiving
pension.
• Statement (c) is correct. The Ministry of
Social Justice and Empowerment runs a
scheme where indigent people living in
welfare institutions like orphanages are
provided with 15 kg of food grains per person
per month at BPL rates.
• Statement (d) is correct. The Ministry of
Human Resource Development (now
Ministry of Education) provides financial
support for the Mid-Day Meal (MDM)
Scheme, the world’s largest school feeding
program.
Hence, option (a) is the correct answer as the
statement is not correct.
Q2. In the last one decade, which one among the
following sectors has attracted the highest Foreign
Direct Investment inflows into India?
(a) Chemicals other than fertilizers
(b) Services sector
(c) Food processing
(d) Telecommunication

Answer: (b)
• The Services sector in India has consistently
attracted the highest FDI equity inflows.
• According to DIPP data, for the period April
2000–March 2016, the services sector
received about US$ 50.79 billion, around 18%
of total foreign inflows.
• FDI is a major driver of economic growth and
a non-debt source of capital.
• FDI differs from FPI in that it involves direct
investment and control in production or
business.
• While Telecom, Chemicals, and Food
Processing also receive significant FDI, the
Services sector (financial, banking, insurance,
outsourcing, R&D, courier, testing) has led for
a prolonged period.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3. Which of the following is not a recommendation
of the task force on direct taxes under the
chairmanship of Dr Vijay L. Kelkar in the year 2002?
(a) Abolition of Wealth Tax
(b) Increase in the exemption limit of personal income
to Rs 1.20 lakh for widows
(c) Elimination of standard deduction
(d) Exemption from tax on dividends and capital gains
from the listed equity
Answer: (b)
• Two Kelkar Task Forces were set up in
September 2002 for tax simplification and
rationalization.
• Statement (b) is not correct. The Task Force
recommended Rs 1 lakh general exemption
and Rs 1.50 lakh for widows and senior
citizens, not Rs 1.20 lakh for widows.
• (a) Abolition of Wealth Tax was
recommended.
• (c) Elimination of standard deduction was
recommended to simplify tax calculation.
• (d) Exemption from tax on dividends and
capital gains was recommended to promote
investments.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q4. Consider the following statements:
1. The National Housing Bank, the apex
institution of housing finance in India, was set
up as a wholly owned subsidiary of the
Reserve Bank of India.
2. The Small Industries Development Bank of
India was established as a wholly-owned
subsidiary of the Industrial Development Bank
of India.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (c)
• Statement 1 is correct. National Housing
Bank (NHB) was established in July 1988 as a
wholly owned subsidiary of RBI, functioning
as the apex housing finance institution.
• Statement 2 is correct. SIDBI was established
on April 2, 1990 as a wholly owned subsidiary
of IDBI, to promote and finance small-scale
industries.
• Later, NHB was taken over by the
Government (2019) and SIDBI was delinked in
2000, but at the time of the question, both
statements were true.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. Assertion (A): For the first time, India had no
trade deficit in the year 2002-03.
Reason (R): For the first time, India’s exports crossed
worth $ 50 billion in the year 2002-03.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not the
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
Answer: (d)
• Assertion is false. India had a trade deficit of
about US$ 12.9 billion in 2002–03.
• Reason is true. 2002–03 was the first time
India’s exports crossed $50 billion, a
landmark year for trade.

•

Since A is false and R is true → (d) is correct.

Q6. Consider the following statements:
1. The loans disbursed to farmers under Kisan
Credit Card Scheme are covered under
Rashtriya Krishi Beema Yojna of Life Insurance
Corporation of India.
2. The Kisan Credit Card holders are provided
personal accident insurance of Rs 50,000 for
accidental death and Rs 25,000 for permanent
disability.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (b)
• Statement 1 is incorrect. Kisan Credit Card
(KCC) loans are not automatically covered
under Rashtriya Krishi Bima Yojana (RKBY).
RKBY is crop insurance, not life insurance.
• Statement 2 is correct. KCC includes Personal
Accident Insurance: Rs 50,000 for accidental
death, Rs 25,000 for permanent disability
(and up to Rs 50,000 for total disability).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7. Consider the following statements:
1. Regarding the procurement of food grains,
Government of India follows a procurement
target rather than an open-ended
procurement policy.
2. Government of India announces minimum
support prices only for cereals.
3. For distribution under Targeted Public
Distribution System (TPDS), wheat and rice
are issued by the Government of India at
uniform Central issue prices to the
States/Union Territories.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 only
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 3 only
Answer: (d)

•

Statement 1 is incorrect. The Government
follows an open-ended procurement policy
— it buys whatever quantity farmers offer at
MSP.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. MSPs are
announced for cereals, pulses, oilseeds, and
commercial crops.
• Statement 3 is correct. Under TPDS, wheat
and rice are issued at uniform Central Issue
Prices to States/UTs.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (a)
• Statement 1 is correct. NTPC has diversified
beyond thermal into hydro, nuclear, and
renewables.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. PGCIL has not
diversified into telecom as a business vertical.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q9. Consider the following statements:
India continues to be dependent on imports to meet
the requirement of oilseeds in the country because:
1. farmers prefer to grow food grains with highly
remunerative support prices.
2. most of the cultivation of oilseed crops
continues to be dependent on rainfall.
3. oils from the seeds of tree origin and rice bran
have remained unexploited.
4. it is far cheaper to import oilseeds than to
cultivate the oilseed crops.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 1, 2 and 3

(c) 3 and 4
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (b)
• Statement 1 is correct. Farmers prefer food
grains with high MSPs.
• Statement 2 is correct. Oilseed cultivation is
largely rainfed, making it riskier.
• Statement 3 is correct. Oils from tree seeds
and rice bran remain under-exploited.
• Statement 4 is incorrect. Import dependency
is due to supply-demand gap, not necessarily
lower cost.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q12. Consider the following statements:
1. The Oil Pool Account of Government of India
was dismantled with effect from 1.4.2002.

are borne by Consolidated Fund of India.
3. An expert committee headed by Dr R.A.
Mashelkar to formulate a national auto fuel
policy recommended that Bharat Stage-II

(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 3

Q13. Which one of the following is the correct
decreasing sequence in terms of the value (in rupees)
of the minerals produced in India in the year 2002-03
(a) Metallic minerals—Fuel minerals—Non metallic
minerals
(b) Fuel minerals—Metallic minerals—Non metallic
minerals
(c) Metallic minerals—Non-metallic minerals— Fuel
minerals

(d) Fuel minerals—Non-metallic minerals— Metallic
minerals
Answer: (b )
• Fuel minerals (coal, lignite, petroleum,
natural gas) account for the largest share of
value.
• Metallic minerals (iron ore, bauxite, copper,
zinc) are second.
• Non-metallic minerals (limestone, dolomite,
mica) have the lowest share.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2006
Q1. Consider the following statement:
1. The nation-wide scheme of the National Child
Labour Projects (NCLP) is run by Union Ministry of
Social Justice and Empowerment.
2. Gurupadswamy Committee dealt with the issue of
child labour.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (b)
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The National Child
Labour Projects (NCLP) scheme is run by
the Ministry of Labour & Employment, not
the Ministry of Social Justice and
Empowerment. The scheme aims to
rehabilitate children withdrawn from
employment by providing them with
education and vocational training.
• Statement 2 is correct. The Gurupadswamy
Committee, formed in 1979, was the first
committee to comprehensively study the
issue of child labour in India. Its
recommendations were instrumental in the
formulation of the Child Labour (Prohibition
& Regulation) Act, 1986.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. Tarapore Committee was associated with which
one of the following?
(a) Special Economic Zones
(b) Fuller capital account convertibility
(c) Foreign exchange reserves
(d) Effect of oil-prices on the Indian economy
Answer: (b)
• The Tarapore Committee, headed by former
RBI Deputy Governor S.S. Tarapore, was
constituted by the Reserve Bank of India
in 1997 to lay down a roadmap for Capital
Account Convertibility (CAC).
• Capital Account Convertibility refers to the
freedom to convert local financial assets into
foreign financial assets and vice versa at
market-determined exchange rates.
• The committee recommended a phased
approach to achieving fuller CAC, contingent
on fulfilling certain preconditions like a low
fiscal deficit, controlled inflation, and a
strengthened financial sector.
• A second Tarapore Committee was also
constituted in 2006 to revisit the roadmap for
fuller CAC.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3. Who among the following served as the Chief
Economist of the International Monetary Fund?
(a) Ashok Lahiri
(b) Sumantra Ghoshal
(c) Saumitra Chaudhuri
(d) Raghuram Rajan
Answer: (d)
• Raghuram Rajan, who later served as the
23rd Governor of the Reserve Bank of India,
was the Chief Economist and Director of
Research at the International Monetary Fund
(IMF) from 2003 to 2006.
• He is a distinguished economist known for his
work in banking, corporate finance, and
economic development.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

•

Q4. Participatory Notes (PNs) are associated with
which one of the following?
(a) Consolidated Fund of India
(b) Foreign Institutional Investors
(c) United Nations Development Programme
(d) Kyoto Protocol
Answer: (b)
• Participatory Notes (PNs), also known as Pnotes or Offshore Derivative Instruments
(ODIs), are financial instruments used by
investors or funds that are not registered with
the Securities and Exchange Board of India
(SEBI) to invest in Indian securities.
• They are issued by registered Foreign
Institutional Investors (FIIs) or their subaccounts to overseas investors.
• PNs allow these investors to gain exposure to
the Indian stock market without registering
directly with the market regulator.
• The use of PNs has been a subject of debate
due to concerns about anonymity and the
potential for money laundering, leading to
stricter regulations by SEBI.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. Which one of the following is the correct
sequence in the decreasing order of contribution of
different sectors to the Gross Domestic Product of
India?
(a) Service- Industry- Agriculture
(b) Service-Agriculture-Industry
(c) Industry- Services-Agriculture
(d) Industry-Agriculture-Services
Answer: (a)

The Services sector has emerged as
the largest and fastest-growing sector,
contributing the most to the Gross Domestic
Product (GDP).
• The Industry sector, which includes
manufacturing, construction, and mining, is
the second-largest contributor.
• The Agriculture sector now has the smallest
share in the GDP, although it still employs a
large portion of the population.
• Therefore, the correct decreasing order of
contribution to India's GDP is Services >
Industry > Agriculture.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q6. With reference to the steel industry in India in
the recent times, consider the following statements:
1. Vizag Steel Plant (RINL) has been declared
Navratna.
2. Merger of IISCO with SAIL has been completed.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (c)
• Statement 1 is correct. Rashtriya Ispat Nigam
Limited (RINL), the corporate entity of the
Visakhapatnam Steel Plant (VSP), was
granted Navratna status on November 17,
2010. This status gives greater autonomy to
Public Sector Undertakings (PSUs).
• Statement 2 is correct. The Indian Iron and
Steel Company (IISCO), a subsidiary of
the Steel Authority of India Limited (SAIL),
was merged with its parent company. The
merger became effective on February 16,
2006.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. Basel II relates to which one of the following?
(a) International standards for safety in civil aviation
(b) Measures against cyber crimes
(c) Measures against drug abuse by sports persons

(d) International standards for measuring the
adequacy of a bank’s capital
Answer: (d)
• Basel II, the second accord, is an international
standard requiring financial institutions to
maintain enough capital reserves to cover the
risks from their operations.
• The accord is based on three pillars: minimum
capital requirements, supervisory review,
and market discipline. Its goal is to enhance
the stability of the international financial
system.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q8. The National Housing Bank was set up in India as

(a) State Bank of India
(b) Reserve Bank of India
(c) ICICI Bank
(d) Life Insurance Corporation of India
Answer: (b)
The National Housing Bank (NHB) was established
on July 9, 1988, under the National Housing Bank Act,
1987.
• It was set up as an apex financial
institution for the housing sector.
• From its inception, the NHB was a whollyowned subsidiary of the Reserve Bank of
India (RBI).

•

(Note: In 2019, the Government of
India acquired the RBI's entire stake, making
NHB a fully government-owned entity).
Hence, option (b) is correct.

3. Y.B. Chavan
4. Pranab Mukherjee

below:
(a) 1, 2 and 3 only
(b) 1, 3 and 4 only
(c) 2 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (d)
• All four individuals listed have served as
the Union Finance Minister of India.
• Y.B. Chavan served as Finance Minister
from 1971 to 1975.
• R. Venkataraman held the office from 1980 to

• Pranab Mukherjee had multiple tenures,

Q10. Which one of the following pairs of countries
joined the European Union in January, 2007?
(a) Bulgaria and Romania
(b) Bulgaria and Belgium
(c) Romania and Slovenia
(d) Hungary and Croatia
Answer: (a)

was followed by the accession of Bulgaria and

countries into the EU.

Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q11. Consider the following statements:
1. The repo rate is the rate at which other banks
borrow from the Reserve Bank of India.
2. A value of 1 for Gini Coefficient in a country implies
that there is perfectly equal income for everyone in its
population.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (a)
• Statement 1 is correct. The repo rate is a key
monetary policy tool of the Reserve Bank of
India (RBI). It is the rate at which the RBI
lends money to commercial banks.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The Gini
coefficient is a measure of inequality. Its
value ranges from 0 to 1. A Gini coefficient
of 0 represents perfect equality, while a value
of 1 represents perfect inequality.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q12. MCA-21 is a major initiative taken up by the
Government of India in which one of the following
areas?
(a) Foreign direct investment in India
(b) Attaching international tourists
(c) e-governance
(d) Modernization of airports
Answer: (c)
• MCA-21 is an e-governance initiative of
the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA).
• Launched in 2006, the project was designed
to automate and streamline processes
related to the administration and regulation
of companies under the Companies Act.
• It enables corporations to file documents
electronically and provides the public with
easy and secure access to corporate
information.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 2008
Q1. What does S & P 500 relate to?
(a) Supercomputer
(b) A new technique in e-business
(c) A new technique in bridge building
(d) An index of stocks of large companies
Answer: (d)
• The S&P 500, or the Standard & Poor's 500, is
a stock market index that represents the
performance of 500 of the largest publiclytraded companies in the United States.
• It is a market-capitalization-weighted index,
meaning companies with larger market caps
have a greater impact on its value.
• The S&P 500 is widely regarded as one of the
best gauges of large-cap U.S. equities and a
common benchmark for the overall health of
the U.S. stock market.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. Which of the following are the public sector
undertakings of the Government of India?
1. Balmer Lawrie and Company Ltd.
2. Dredging Corporation of India 3. Educational
Consultants of India Ltd.
Select the correct answer using the code given
below:
(a) 1 and 2 Only
(b) 2 and 3 Only
(c) 1 and 3 Only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (d)
• All three entities listed are Public Sector
Undertakings (PSUs) under the Government
of India.
• Balmer Lawrie and Company Ltd. is
a Miniratna I public sector enterprise under
the Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas.
• Dredging Corporation of India (DCI) is
a Miniratna public sector enterprise under
the Ministry of Shipping, providing dredging
services to major ports.
• Educational Consultants of India Ltd.
(EdCIL) is a Miniratna public sector enterprise

under the Ministry of Education, offering
consultancy in the education sector.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. How is the United Nations Monetary and
financial Conference wherein the agreements were
signed to set up IBRD, GATT and IMF, commonly
known?
(a) Bandung Conference
(b) Bretton Woods Conference
(c) Versailles Conference
(d) Yalta Conference
Answer: (b)
• The United Nations Monetary and Financial
Conference, held in July 1944 in Bretton
Woods, New Hampshire, USA, is commonly
known as the Bretton Woods Conference.
• Delegates from 44 Allied nations gathered to
establish a new international economic
order for the post-World War II world.
• Key outcomes were the creation of
the International Monetary Fund (IMF) and
the International Bank for Reconstruction
and Development (IBRD) (now part of the
World Bank). It also laid the groundwork for
the General Agreement on Tariffs and Trade
(GATT).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. Which of the following pairs about India’s
economic indicator and agricultural production (all in
rounded figures) are correctly matched?
1. GDP per capita (current prices) : Rs 37,000
2. Rice : 180 million tons
3. Wheat : 75 million tons
Select the correct answer using the code given
below:
(a) 1, 2 and 3
(b) 1 and 2 Only
(c) 2 and 3 Only
(d) 1 and 3 Only
Answer: (d)
• Pair 1 is correctly matched. For the period
around 2007-08, India's GDP per capita at
current prices was approximately Rs 37,000.

•

Pair 2 is incorrectly matched. India's rice
production in 2007-08 was around 93.4
million tons, not 180 million tons.
• Pair 3 is correctly matched. India's wheat
production in 2007-08 was
approximately 75.8 million tons, which
rounds to 75 million tons.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q5. In August 2006, the Government of India notified
the Rural Electrification Policy. This Policy aims at
provision of access to all household by which year?
(a) 2008
(b) 2009
(c) 2010
(d) 2012
Answer: (b)
• The National Rural Electrification Policy,
notified in August 2006, was a major
government initiative to expand electricity
access in rural India.
• The policy set a clear goal to provide access to
electricity to all households across the
country by the year 2009.
• This was a key objective under the
broader Bharat Nirman program.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. As per India’s National Population Policy, 2000,
by which one of the following years is it our longterm objective to achieve population stabilization?
(a) 2025
(b) 2035
(c) 2045
(d) 2055
Answer: (c)
• The National Population Policy (NPP),
2000 outlined a comprehensive framework to
address India's population challenges.
• The policy set a medium-term objective to
bring the Total Fertility Rate (TFR) to
replacement levels by 2010.
• The long-term objective of the NPP, 2000 is to
achieve a stable population by the year 2045.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q7. Which one of the following Union Ministries is

Ministry)?
(a) Ministry of Agriculture
(b) Ministry of Science and Technology
(c) Ministry of New and Renewable Energy
(d) Ministry of Rural Development
Answer: (d)
• The National Biodiesel Mission was launched
in 2003 to promote biodiesel as an alternative
fuel.
• The Ministry of Rural Development was
designated as the nodal ministry for its
implementation.
• This was because the mission relied on the
cultivation of non-edible oilseed plants,
like Jatropha, on wasteland, which is linked
to rural employment and development.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q8. Consider the following statements with
reference to Indira Gandhi National Old Age Pension

1. All persons of 60 years or above belonging to the

(b) 2 Only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2

• Statement 1 is incorrect. The Indira Gandhi
National Old Age Pension Scheme
(IGNOAPS) is applicable to individuals aged
60+ from households below the poverty line
(BPL) in both rural and urban areas.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. At that time, the
central assistance was Rs. 200 per month for
ages 60-79 and Rs. 500 per month for ages 80

and above. The amount of Rs. 300 is not
correct.

skills to women to enable them to find

• The scheme provides financial assistance for
training in traditional and non-traditional
trades, promoting women's economic
empowerment.

Q1. Consider the following statements:
1. Infant mortality rate takes into account the death
of infants within a month after birth.
2. Infant mortality rate is the number of infant deaths
in a particular year per 100 live births during that
year.
Which of the above statements is/ are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (d)
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The Infant Mortality
Rate (IMR) is defined as the number of deaths
of children under one year of age. It does not
just take into account deaths within a month

after birth (which is the neonatal mortality
rate).
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The Infant Mortality
Rate is calculated as the number of infant
deaths per 1,000 live births, not per 100 live
births.
Hence, option (d) is correct as both statements are
incorrect.

the Ministry of Labour and Employment.
• Statement 2 is correct. The Rajiv Gandhi
Shilpi Swasthya Bima Yojana (RGSSBY) is a
health insurance scheme for artisans and
their families, launched and implemented by
the Ministry of Textiles.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. With which one of the following has the B.K.
Chaturvedi Committee dealt?
(a) Review of Centre-state relations
(b) Review of Delimitation Act
(c) Tax reforms and measures to increase revenues
(d) Price reform in the oil sector
Answer: (d)
• The B.K. Chaturvedi Committee was
constituted in 2008 to examine the financial
health of oil marketing companies (OMCs) in
India.
• The committee was tasked with reviewing the
existing pricing mechanism for petroleum
products and suggesting reforms.
• Its recommendations included
the deregulation of petrol and diesel
prices and a more targeted subsidy
mechanism for kerosene and LPG.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

(a) Third
(b) Fourth
(c) Fifth
(d) Sixth
Answer: (c)
• The Fifth Five Year Plan spanned the period
from 1974 to 1979.
• The Internal Emergency was declared in June
1975 and lasted until March 1977, which was
during the Fifth Plan.
• Following the Emergency, general elections
were held in 1977, which resulted in
the Janata Party being elected to power.
• The new Janata government terminated the
Fifth Five Year Plan one year ahead of
schedule in 1978.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q5. Which one of the following brings out the

Answer: (d)

• It brings out a variety of statistical
publications, including the annual “Energy
Statistics”.

•

This publication provides a comprehensive
overview of the energy sector in India,
covering data on reserves, production,
consumption, and prices.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q7. Consider the following statements:

(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (d)
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The Commission for

(CACP) recommends Minimum Support Prices
(MSPs) for 23 commodities, not 32.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The National Food

2007 by the Ministry of Agriculture, not the
