Q8. In the context of independent India’s Economy,
which one of the following was the earliest event to
take place?
(a) Nationalisation of Insurance companies
(b) Nationalisation of State Bank of India
(c) Enactment of Banking Regulation Act
(d) Introduction of First Five-Year Plan
Answer: (c)
• To understand the correct chronological
order, let's look at the dates of these events:
• Enactment of Banking Regulation
Act: This act was passed in 1949.
• Introduction of First Five-Year
Plan: This was launched in 1951.
• Nationalisation of State Bank of
India: This occurred in 1955 by
nationalizing the Imperial Bank of
India.
• Nationalisation of Insurance
companies: The life insurance sector
was nationalized in 1956 with the
formation of LIC.
• Based on these dates, the enactment of
the Banking Regulation Act in 1949 was
the earliest of the four events.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q9. Which one of the following pairs is not correctly
matched?
(a) Japan: Nikkei
(b) Singapore: Shcomp
(c) UK : FISE
(d) USA : Nasdaq
Answer: (b)
• This question tests knowledge of major
international stock market indices.
• (a) Japan: Nikkei is a correct match.
The Nikkei 225 is the leading index for the
Tokyo Stock Exchange.
• (b) Singapore: Shcomp is not a correct match.
The main index for Singapore is the Straits
Times Index (STI). Shcomp refers to
the Shanghai Composite Index (China).
• (c) UK : FISE is a correct match. The FTSE 100
Index is the primary index for the London
Stock Exchange.
• (d) USA : Nasdaq is a correct match.
The Nasdaq Composite is a major stock index
in the United States.
Hence, option (b) is not correctly matched.
Q10. Consider the following:
1. Fringe Benefit Tax
2. Interest Tax
3. Securities Transaction Tax
Which of the above is/ are Direct Tax/Taxes?
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 3 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1,2 and 3
Answer: (d)
• Direct taxes are levied directly on the income
or wealth of an individual or entity.
• Fringe Benefit Tax (FBT): This was a tax paid
by employers on benefits provided to
employees. It was a direct tax on the
employer.
• Interest Tax: This was a tax on the interest
income of lending institutions, making it
a direct tax on their income.
• Securities Transaction Tax (STT): This is a tax
levied on the purchase and sale of securities.

It is a direct tax collected from the transacting
parties.
• Since all three are levied directly on the
taxpayer, they are all considered direct taxes.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q11. Consider the following statements regarding
Indian Planning:
1. The Second Five-Year Plan emphasized on the
establishment of heavy industries.
2. The Third Five-Year Plan introduced the concept of
import substitution as a strategy for industrialization.
Which of the statements given above is/ are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (c)
• Statement 1 is correct. The Second Five-Year
Plan (1956-61), based on the Mahalanobis
model, placed a strong emphasis on heavy
industries and capital goods.
• Statement 2 is correct. The Third Five-Year
Plan (1961-66) aimed for a self-reliant
economy and introduced import
substitution (replacing foreign imports with
domestic production) as a key strategy.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2010
Q1. Which of the following terms indicates a
mechanism used by commercial banks for providing
credit to the government?
(a) Cash Credit Ratio
(b) Debt Service Obligation
(c) Liquidity Adjustment Facility
(d) Statutory Liquidity Ratio
Answer: (d)
• Statutory Liquidity Ratio (SLR) is a
requirement mandated by the Reserve Bank
of India (RBI) for all commercial banks.
• It is the minimum percentage of a bank's Net
Demand and Time Liabilities (NDTL) that it
must maintain in the form of liquid assets,

including cash, gold, and governmentapproved securities.
• By mandating that banks invest
in government securities, the SLR mechanism
effectively serves as a way for commercial
banks to provide credit to the government,
ensuring a captive market for government
debt.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. The SEZ Act, 2005 which came, into effect in
February 2006 has certain objectives. In this context,
consider the following:
1. Development of infrastructure facilities.
2. Promotion of investment from foreign sources.
3. Promotion of exports of services only.
Which of the above are the objectives of this Act?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (a)
• The Special Economic Zones (SEZ) Act,
2005 was enacted to create a stable policy
framework for SEZs in India.
• Objective 1 is correct. A primary goal is
the development of world-class
infrastructure facilities within these zones.
• Objective 2 is correct. A key objective is
the promotion of investment from
both domestic and foreign sources.
• Objective 3 is incorrect. The Act aims to
promote the export of both goods and
services, not just services only.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. Which one of the following statements is an
appropriate description of deflation?
(a) It is a sudden fall in the value of a currency against
other currencies
(b) It is a persistent recession in both the financial and
real sectors of economy
(c) It is a persistent fall in the general price level of
goods and services

(d) It is a fall in the rate of inflation over a period of
time
Answer: (c)
• Deflation is an economic term describing a
sustained and persistent decrease in the
general price level of goods and services,
where the inflation rate becomes negative.
• This means money can buy more over time,
but it is often associated with economic
problems like reduced business revenues,
falling wages, and increased unemployment.
• It is the opposite of inflation (a persistent
price increase). Disinflation is a fall in the rate
of inflation, but the rate remains positive.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4. With reference to the Non-banking Financial
Companies (NBFCs) in India, consider the following
statements:
1. They cannot engage in the acquisition of securities
issued by the government.
2. They cannot accept demand deposits like Savings
Account.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (b)
• Non-Banking Financial Companies
(NBFCs) are companies engaged in financial
activities but do not have a full banking
license.
• Statement 1 is incorrect. A primary activity of
many NBFCs is the acquisition of shares,
stocks, bonds, and securities issued by the
government.
• Statement 2 is correct. A key distinction is
that NBFCs cannot accept demand deposits,
meaning they cannot offer savings or current
accounts. They are also not part of the
payment and settlement system.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q5. Which one of the following was not stipulated in
the Fiscal Responsibility and Budget Management
Act, 2003?
(a) Elimination of revenue deficit by the end of the
fiscal year 2007-08
(b) Non-borrowing by the central government from
Reserve Bank of India except under certain
circumstances
(c) Elimination of primary deficit by the end of the
fiscal year 2008-09
(d) Fixing government guarantees in any financial year
as a percentage
Answer: (c)
• The Fiscal Responsibility and Budget
Management (FRBM) Act, 2003 was enacted
to institutionalize fiscal discipline.
• The Act mandated the elimination of the
revenue deficit and prohibited the central
government from borrowing directly from
the RBI (except in specific cases).
• It also required the government to
place limits on the guarantees it could give.
• However, the FRBM Act did not stipulate any
target for the elimination of the primary
deficit. The focus was on the revenue and
fiscal deficits.
Hence, option (c) was not a stipulation of the Act.
Q6. In the parlance of financial investments, the
term ‘bear’ denotes
(a) An investor who feels that the price of a particular
security is going to fall
(b) An investor who expects the price of particular
shares to rise
(c) A shareholder or a bondholder who has an interest
in a company, Financial or otherwise
(d) Any lender whether by making a loan or buying a
bond
Answer: (a)
• In financial markets, a ‘bear’ is an investor
who is pessimistic and expects the prices of
securities to decline.
• This bearish sentiment leads them to sell
securities, hoping to buy them back later at a
lower price.

•

A ‘bear market’ is a period of falling stock
prices. The opposite is a ‘bull’, who is an
optimistic investor.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q7. In the context of Indian economy, consider the
following pairs:
Term

Most appropriate description

1. Melt down Fall in stock prices
2. Recession Fall in growth rate
3. Slow down Fall in GDP
Which of the pairs given above is/are correctly
matched?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (a)
• Pair 1 is correctly matched.
A meltdown refers to a sudden and severe
drop in stock prices.
• Pair 2 is incorrectly matched. A recession is
technically defined as two consecutive
quarters of negative GDP growth (a fall in
GDP), not just a fall in the growth rate.
• Pair 3 is incorrectly matched.
A slowdown refers to a fall in the rate of
economic growth (GDP is still growing, but
slower). A fall in GDP is a contraction or
recession.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q8. Which of the following is/are treated as artificial
currency?
(a) ADR
(b) GDR
(c) SDR
(d) Both ADR and SDR
Answer: (c)
• Special Drawing Rights (SDRs) are an
international reserve asset created by
the International Monetary Fund (IMF) and
are often called ‘paper gold’ or ‘artificial
currency’.

•

SDRs are not a currency but a potential claim
on the freely usable currencies of IMF
members, with their value based on a basket
of major currencies.
• ADRs (American Depositary
Receipts) and GDRs (Global Depositary
Receipts) are certificates representing shares
in a foreign company and are not artificial
currencies.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q9. In India, the interest rate on savings accounts in
all the nationalized commercial banks is fixed by
(a) Union Ministry of Finance
(b) Union Finance Commission
(c) Indian Banks’ Association
(d) None of the above
Answer: (d)
• Prior to 2011, the interest rate on savings
accounts was regulated by the Reserve Bank
of India (RBI).
• In October 2011, the RBI deregulated the
savings bank deposit interest rate.
• Therefore, the interest rate is not fixed by any
central authority but is determined by each
individual bank.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q10. Sustainable development is described as the
development that meets the needs of the present
without compromising the ability of future
generations to meet their own needs. In this
perspective, inherently the concept of sustainable
development is intertwined with which of the
following concepts?
(a) Social justice and empowerment
(b) Inclusive Growth
(c) Globalization
(d) Carrying capacity
Answer: (d)
Sustainable development is about balancing current
human needs with the preservation of the
environment for future generations.
• This is inherently linked to the concept
of carrying capacity, which is the maximum
population that can be sustained by an
environment's resources.
• Sustainable development implies that human
activities should not exceed the carrying
capacity of the Earth’s ecosystems to ensure
resources are available for the future.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q11. In the context of governance, consider the
following:
1. Encouraging Foreign Direct Investment inflows
2. Privatization of higher educational Institutions
3. Down-sizing of bureaucracy
4. Selling/offloading the shares of Public Sector
Undertakings
Which of the above can be used as measures to
control the fiscal deficit in India?
(a) 1,2 and 3
(b) 2, 3 and 4
(c) 1, 2 and 4
(d) 3 and 4 only
Answer: (d)
• To control the fiscal deficit, the government
must either reduce expenditure or increase
non-borrowed receipts.
• Encouraging FDI inflows finances the deficit
but does not reduce it.

•

Privatization of educational institutions is not
a direct measure to control the current fiscal
deficit.
• Down-sizing of bureaucracy is a measure
to reduce government expenditure by
lowering the wage bill.
• Selling shares of PSUs (disinvestment) is a
way to increase the government's non-debt
capital receipts.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q12. Inclusive growth as enunciated in the Eleventh
Five Year Plan does not include one of the following:
(a) Reduction of poverty
(b) Extension of employment opportunities
(c) Strengthening of capital market
(d) Reduction of gender inequality
Answer: (c)
• The Eleventh Five Year Plan (2007-2012) had
the theme of “faster and more inclusive
growth.”
• Inclusive growth focuses on ensuring the
benefits of growth are shared by all, and its
key pillars include poverty
reduction, employment generation, gender
equality, and improving access to essential
services.
• While a strong capital market is important for
the economy, its strengthening was not a
direct component of the concept of inclusive
growth, which is more focused on social
equity.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q13. Consider the following actions by the
Government:
1. Cutting the tax rates
2. Increasing the government spending
3. Abolishing the subsidies
In the context of economic recession, which of the
above actions can be considered a part of the “fiscal
stimulus” package?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 1 and 3 only

(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (a)
• A fiscal stimulus is a set of government
measures to boost a slowing economy by
increasing aggregate demand.
• Cutting tax rates is a key component, as it
increases disposable income, encouraging
spending and investment.
• Increasing government spending directly
injects money into the economy, creating jobs
and boosting demand.
• Abolishing subsidies is a measure of fiscal
consolidation (austerity), not stimulus, as it
reduces government spending.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q14. When the Reserve Bank of India announces an
increase of the Cash Reserve Ratio, what does it
mean?
(a) The commercial banks will have less money to lend
(b) The Reserve Bank of India will have less money to
lend
(c) The Union Government will have less money to
lend
(d) The commercial banks will have more money to
lend
Answer: (a)
• The Cash Reserve Ratio (CRR) is the
percentage of a bank's deposits that it must
keep as cash reserves with the Reserve Bank
of India (RBI).
• When the RBI increases the CRR, banks have
to set aside a larger portion of their deposits.
• This reduces the amount of money
available with the banks for lending and
investment, thereby reducing liquidity in the
banking system.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q15. With reference to India, consider the following
statements:
1. The Wholesale Price Index (WPI) in India is available
on a monthly basis only.

2. As compared to Consumer Price Index for Industrial
Workers (CPI(IW)), the WPI gives less weight to food
articles.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (b)
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The Wholesale Price
Index (WPI) data is released on a weekly
basis for primary articles and fuel, and the
final comprehensive data is released monthly.
• Statement 2 is correct. The WPI gives a higher
weightage to manufactured goods and
a relatively lower weightage to food articles.
In contrast, the Consumer Price Index
(CPI) gives a much higher weightage to food
articles.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q16. In the context of India’s Five Year Plans, a shift
in the pattern of industrialization, with lower
emphasis on heavy industries and more on
infrastructure begins in
(a) Fourth Plan
(b) Sixth Plan
(c) Eighth Plan
(d) Tenth Plan
Answer: (c)
• Early Five-Year Plans prioritized heavy
industries.
• A significant shift occurred during the Eighth
Five-Year Plan (1992-97), launched after
the 1991 economic reforms.
• The Eighth Plan moved away from the heavy
industry focus and placed a much greater
emphasis on the development
of infrastructure, human development, and
social sectors.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q17. In India, which of the following is regulated by’
the Forward Markets Commission?
(a) Currency Futures Trading

(b) Commodities Futures Trading
(c) Equity Futures Trading
(d) Both Commodities Futures and Financial Futures
Trading
Answer: (b)
• The Forward Markets Commission (FMC) was
the chief regulator of the commodities
futures market in India.
• It was a statutory body established in 1953 to
regulate and oversee commodity exchanges.
• (Note: In September 2015, the FMC was
merged with the Securities and Exchange
Board of India (SEBI), which is now the unified
regulator).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q18. Which one of the following is not a feature of
Limited Liability Partnership firm?
(a) Partners should be less than 20
(b) Partnership and management need not be
separate
(c) Internal governance may be decided by mutual
agreement among partners
(d) It is corporate body with perpetual succession
Answer: (a)
• A Limited Liability Partnership (LLP) combines
features of a partnership and a company.
• (a) is not a feature. An LLP requires a
minimum of two partners but has no upper
limit on the number of partners, unlike a
traditional partnership.
• (b), (c), and (d) are features: partners can
manage the firm, internal governance is by
mutual agreement, and it is a body corporate
with perpetual succession.
Hence, option (a) is not a feature of an LLP.
Q19. With reference to the institution of Banking
Ombudsman in India, which one of the statements is
not correct?
(a) The Banking Ombudsman is appointed by the
Reserve Bank of India
(b) The Banking Ombudsman can consider complaints
from Non- Resident Indians having accounts in India

(c) The orders passed by the Banking Ombudsman are
final and binding on the parties concerned
(d) The service provided by the Banking Ombudsman
is free of any fee
Answer: (c)
The Banking Ombudsman Scheme is a forum for bank
customers to resolve complaints.
• (a) is correct. The Ombudsman is appointed
by the RBI.
• (b) is correct. The scheme is available to all
customers, including NRIs.
• (c) is not correct. The orders are not final and
binding. An appeal can be made to
the Appellate Authority (a Deputy Governor
of the RBI) by either the customer or the
bank.
• (d) is correct. The service is free of cost.
Hence, option (c) is the incorrect statement.
Q20. With reference to India, consider the following:
1. Nationalization of Banks
2. Formation of Regional
Rural Banks
3. Adoption of villages by Bank Branches
Which of the above can be considered as steps taken
to achieve the “financial inclusion” in India?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (d)
• Financial inclusion is the process of ensuring
access to financial services for all sections of
society.
• Nationalization of Banks (1969, 1980) aimed
to expand the banking network into rural
areas.
• Formation of Regional Rural Banks (RRBs)
(1975) was specifically to cater to the credit
needs of the rural population.
• Adoption of villages by Bank Branches
(Service Area Approach, 1989) aimed to
improve the delivery of credit in designated
rural areas.

•

All three were major steps towards achieving
financial inclusion.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q21. The functions of commercial banks in India
include
1. Purchase and sale of shares and securities on behalf
of customers
2. Acting as executors and trustees of wills.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (c)
• Commercial banks perform primary and
secondary functions. The functions listed
are agency functions (secondary).
• 1 is correct. Banks act as agents for customers
by undertaking the purchase and sale of
shares and securities.
• 2 is correct. Banks can also provide trustee
services, acting as executors to manage a
person's estate or as trustees to manage
assets.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q22. In India, the tax proceeds of which one of the
following as a percentage of gross tax revenue has
significantly declined in the last five years?
(a) Service tax
(b) Personal income tax
(c) Excise duty
(d) Corporation tax
Answer: (c)
• This question refers to the trend in tax
revenue in the years leading up to 2010.
• Excise duty, a tax on manufacturing, was
historically a major revenue source, but its
share in gross tax revenue had been on
a declining trend.
• This was due to rate reductions (as part of
fiscal stimulus) and the economy's structural
shift towards the services sector.

•

In contrast, the shares of Corporation tax,
Personal income tax, and Service tax were
generally increasing.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q23. In India, taxes on transactions in Stock
Exchanges and Futures Markets are
1. levied by the Union
2. collected by the States
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (a)
• Taxes on stock exchange transactions, like
the Securities Transaction Tax (STT), fall
under the authority of the Union
Government.
• Statement 1 is correct. The power
to levy these taxes is vested with the Union
(Central) Government.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. These taxes are not
only levied but also collected by the Union
Government. The proceeds go to the
Consolidated Fund of India.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q24. With reference to Indian economy, consider the
following statements:
1. The Gross Domestic Product (GDP) has increased by
four times in the last 10 years.
2. The percentage share of Public Sector in GDP has
declined in the last 10 years.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (b)
• This question refers to the period from
roughly 2000 to 2010.
• Statement 1 is incorrect. While India's GDP
grew significantly, it increased by

approximately three times, not four times,
during this decade.
• Statement 2 is correct. Following the 1991
economic reforms, the private sector grew
much faster than the public sector, leading to
a decline in the percentage share of the
Public Sector in the country's overall GDP.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2011
Q1. Regarding the International Monetary Fund,
which one of the following statements is correct?
(a) It can grant loans to any country.
(b) It can grant loans to only developed countries.
(c) It grants loans to only member countries.
(d) It can grant loans to the central bank of a country.
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• The International Monetary Fund (IMF) is a
global organization of 189 member countries.
• One of its core functions is to provide
financial assistance to its member countries
that are experiencing actual or potential
balance of payments problems.
• This lending is conditional on the borrowing
country implementing policies to correct the
underlying economic issues.
• The IMF does not grant loans to any country
indiscriminately; the recipient must be a
member of the IMF.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. India has experienced persistent and high food
inflation in the recent past. What could be the
reasons?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• Statement 1 is incorrect. While there has
been some shift towards commercial crops, a
steady decrease of 30% in the area under

•

•

•

food grains in five years is a significant
exaggeration. The decline has been much
more modest.
Statement 2 is correct. With rising incomes,
there has been a notable shift in
consumption patterns. People are moving
from basic staples towards higher-value
foods like fruits, vegetables, milk, eggs, and
meat. This increased demand contributes to
food inflation.
Statement 3 is correct. India's food supply
chain suffers from significant structural
constraints. These include a lack of adequate
storage facilities, inefficient logistics, and
multiple intermediaries, which lead to high
wastage and increased costs.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q3. In terms of economy, the visit by foreign
nationals to witness the XIX common wealth games
in India amounted to
(a) Exports
(b) Imports
(c) Production
(d) Consumption
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• In economics, an export is the sale of goods
and services produced in one country to
residents of another country.
• When foreign nationals visit a country, their
spending on locally produced goods and
services represents an inflow of foreign
currency.
• This spending is treated as an export of
services (in this case, tourism and hospitality
services) and contributes positively to the
country's balance of payments.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. Which one of the following statements
appropriately describes the “fiscal stimulus”?
(a) It is a massive investment by the government in
manufacturing sector to ensure the supply of goods to
meet the demand surge caused by rapid economic

growth.
(b) It is an intense affirmative action of the
government to boost economic activity in the country.
(c) It is government’s intensive action on financial
institutions to ensure disbursement of loans to
agriculture and allied sectors to promote greater food
production and contain food inflation.
(d) It is an extreme affirmative action by the
government to pursue its policy of financial inclusion
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• A fiscal stimulus refers to policy measures
undertaken by a government to boost a
flagging economy, typically during a
recession.
• The primary goal is to increase aggregate
demand and stimulate economic activity.
• This is achieved through two main channels:
Increased government spending and Tax
cuts.
• Therefore, it is an intense affirmative action
by the government designed to inject
demand into the economy and encourage
growth.
• Hence, option (b) is the most appropriate
description.
Q5. Consider the following actions which the
Government can take:
1. Devaluing the domestic currency
2. Reduction in the export subsidy
3. Adopting suitable policies which attract
greater FDI and more funds from FIIs
Which of the above action/actions can help in
reducing the current account deficit?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 3 only
(d) 1 and 3
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• The Current Account Deficit (CAD) occurs
when the value of a country's imports is
greater than the value of its exports.

•

•

•

•

Devaluing the domestic currency makes a
country's exports cheaper and its imports
more expensive, which can help reduce the
CAD.
Reduction in the export subsidy would make
exports more expensive and less competitive,
likely worsening the CAD.
Attracting greater FDI and FII inflows
increases the supply of foreign currency and
helps in financing the CAD, easing pressure on
the external sector.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q6. A rapid increase in the rate of inflation is
sometimes attributed to the “base effect”. What is
“base effect”?
(a) It is the impact of drastic deficiency in supply due
to failure of crops.
(b) It is the impact of the surge in demand due to
rapid economic growth
(c) It is the impact of the price levels of previous year
on the calculation of inflation rate
(d) None of the statements (a), (b) and (c) given above
is correct in this context
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• The base effect is a statistical phenomenon.
Inflation is calculated as the percentage
change in the price index from the same
period in the previous year.
• The base effect refers to the influence of the
price level of the corresponding period of the
previous year (the 'base' period) on the
current year's inflation rate.
• If the inflation rate was very low in the base
period, even a small price increase now will
show a high rate of inflation. Conversely, if
the rate was very high in the base period, a
similar increase now will show a lower rate of
inflation.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. India is regarded as a country with
“Demographic Dividend’’. This is due to

(a) Its high population in the age group below 15
years.
(b) Its high population in the age group of 15-64 years.
(c) Its high population in the age group above 65
years.
(d) Its high total population
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• Demographic dividend refers to the potential
for economic growth that can result from
shifts in a population's age structure.
• This dividend occurs when the proportion of
the working-age population (15-64 years) is
large relative to the non-working-age
(dependent) population.
• A larger working-age population means more
people are potentially productive, presenting
a significant opportunity for economic
growth.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q8. Why is the offering of “teaser loans’’ by
commercial banks a cause of economic concern?
1. The teaser loans are considered to be an
aspect of sub-prime lending and banks may be
exposed to the risk of defaulters in future.
2. In India, the teaser loans are mostly given to
inexperienced entrepreneurs to set up
manufacturing or export units.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• Teaser loans offer a low, fixed introductory
interest rate for a short period, after which
the rate increases.
• Statement 1 is correct. The concern is that
they can be a form of sub-prime lending.
Borrowers may be attracted by the low initial
rate but unable to afford the higher rates
later, increasing the risk of default and nonperforming assets (NPAs).

•

•

Statement 2 is incorrect. In India, teaser loans
have been most commonly associated with
home loans, not primarily with loans to
entrepreneurs.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q9. Which amongst the following provided a
common factor for tribal insurrection in India in the
19th century?
(a) Introduction of a new system of land revenue and
taxation of tribal products.
(b) Influence of foreign religious missionaries in tribal
areas.
(c) Rise of a large number of money lenders, traders
and revenue farmers as middlemen in tribal areas.
(d) The complete disruption of the old agrarian order
of the tribal communities.
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• The 19th century saw numerous tribal
uprisings against British rule.
• While factors (a), (b), and (c) were significant
causes, the most comprehensive factor was
the complete disruption of the traditional
socio-economic and agrarian systems of the
tribal communities.
• British policies led to the loss of tribal
autonomy, alienation of their lands, the
influx of outsiders, new taxes, and the
destruction of their traditional livelihoods.
• This holistic disruption was the common
thread.
• Hence, option (d) is the most encompassing
answer.
Q10. In the context of Indian economy, consider the
following statements?
1. The growth rate of GDP has steadily increased
in the last five years.
2. The growth rate in per capita income has
steadily increased in the last five years.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2

(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• This question refers to the period from
roughly 2006 to 2011.
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The growth rate of
India's GDP did not increase steadily. It was
high before the 2008 global financial crisis,
then slowed down, and then recovered. The
growth path was volatile.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The growth rate of
per capita income is closely linked to GDP
growth. Since GDP growth was not steady,
the growth rate of per capita income also
fluctuated.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q11. In India, which of the following have the
highest share in the disbursement of credit to
agriculture and allied activities?
(a) Commercial banks
(b) Cooperative banks
(c) Regional rural banks
(d) Microfinance institutions
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• The formal credit system for agriculture
consists of Commercial Banks, Cooperative
Banks, and Regional Rural Banks (RRBs).
• Over the years, the role of Commercial Banks
in agricultural credit has grown significantly.
• They have the largest share in the total credit
disbursed to the agriculture and allied
sectors.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q12. Which of the following can aid in furthering the
government’s objective of inclusive growth?
1. Promoting self-help groups.
2. Promoting micro, small and medium
enterprises
3. Implementing the right to education act
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:

(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Inclusive growth is about ensuring that the
benefits of economic development reach all
sections of society.
• Promoting Self-Help Groups (SHGs)
empowers the poor, especially women, by
providing access to credit and promoting
financial independence.
• Promoting Micro, Small, and Medium
Enterprises (MSMEs) is crucial for
employment generation.
• Implementing the Right to Education Act
ensures access to quality education, a key
enabler of social mobility and economic
opportunity.
• All three are fundamental pillars for achieving
inclusive growth.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q13. Why is the government of India disinvesting its
equity in the central public sector enterprises
(CPSEs)?
1. The government intends to use the revenue
earned from the disinvestment mainly to pay
back the external debt.
2. The government no longer intends to retain
the management control of the CPSEs.
Which the correct statement given above is/are
correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Disinvestment is the process of the
government selling its shares in Central
Public Sector Enterprises (CPSEs).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The revenue is
primarily used to finance the fiscal deficit and

•

•

to fund social sector and infrastructure
projects, not mainly to pay back external
debt.
Statement 2 is incorrect. The government's
policy has evolved. In many cases, the
government intends to retain management
control (hold at least 51% stake) while selling
a minority stake.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q14. Economic growth is usually coupled with
(a) Deflation
(b) Inflation
(c) Stagflation
(d) Hyperinflation
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• Economic growth leads to an increase in
national income and employment, which
increases aggregate demand.
• If supply does not increase at the same pace,
this excess demand puts upward pressure on
prices, resulting in inflation.
• This is often called demand-pull inflation.
• A moderate level of inflation is often seen as
a natural consequence of a growing
economy.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q15. The lowering of bank rate by the Reserve Bank
of India leads to
(a) More liquidity in the market
(b) Less liquidity in the market.
(c) No change in the liquidity in the market

(d) Mobilization of more deposits by commercial
banks
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• The Bank Rate is the rate at which the
Reserve Bank of India (RBI) lends money to
commercial banks for long-term needs.
• When the RBI lowers the bank rate, it
becomes cheaper for commercial banks to
borrow from the central bank.
• This encourages banks to lower their own
lending rates, leading to increased borrowing
and spending, which in turn increases the
money supply and liquidity in the market.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q16. Which one of the following is not a feature of
“Value Added Tax”?
(a) It is multi-point destination-based system of
taxation.
(b) It is a tax levied on value addition at each stage of
transaction in the production- distribution chain.
(c) It is a tax on the final consumption of goods or
services and must ultimately be borne by the
consumer.
(d) It is basically a subject of the central government
and the state governments are only a facilitator for its
successful implementation
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Value Added Tax (VAT) was a consumption
tax.
• (a), (b), and (c) are correct features. VAT is a
multi-point, destination-based tax levied on
the value added at each stage, with the final
burden borne by the consumer.
• (d) is not a correct feature. In India, before
GST, VAT was a State subject. It was levied
and collected by the State Governments. The
Central Government was only a facilitator.
• Hence, option (d) is the incorrect statement.
Q17. A “closed economy’’ is an economy in which
(a) The money supply is fully controlled
(b) Deficit financing takes place.

(c) Only exports take place.
(d) Neither exports nor imports take place
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• A closed economy is a theoretical concept for
an economy that is self-sufficient and has no
interaction with the outside world.
• This means that it does not engage in
international trade; there are no exports and
no imports.
• In a closed economy, GDP = Consumption +
Investment + Government spending (Y = C + I
+ G).
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q18. Karl Marx explained the process of class
struggle with the help of which one of the following
theories?
(a) Empirical liberalism.
(b) Existentialism.
(c) Darwin’s theory of evolution.
(d) Dialectical materialism
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• The philosophical foundation of Marxism is
dialectical materialism.
• Materialism posits that material conditions
(the economic base of society) are the
primary drivers of social change.
• Dialectics suggests that history progresses
through conflicts.
• Marx applied this to society, arguing that
history is a story of class struggle arising from
contradictions between economic classes.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q19. Microfinance is the provision of financial
services to people of low-income groups. This
includes both the consumers and the self-employed.
The service/ services rendered under micro- finance
is/are:
1. Credit facilities
2. Savings facilities.
3. Insurance facilities.
4. Fund transfer facilities.

Select the correct answer using the codes given the
lists :
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 4 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Microfinance is a broad category of financial
services for low-income individuals who lack
access to traditional banking.
• While it started with microcredit, the concept
has expanded to include a wider range of
services:
o Credit facilities: Small loans.
o Savings facilities: Safe ways to save.
o Insurance facilities: Micro-insurance
products.
o Fund transfer facilities: Services for
remitting money.
• Providing this full range of services helps in
promoting financial inclusion and poverty
alleviation.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q20. Both foreign direct investment (FDI) and foreign
institutional Investor(FII) are related to investment in
a country. Which one of the following statements
best represents an important difference between the
two?
(a) FII helps bring better management skills and
technology. While FDI only brings in capital.
(b) FII helps in increasing capital availability in general,
while FDI only targets specific.
(c) FDI flows only into the secondary market, in
general, while FDI only targets specific sectors.
(d) FII is considered to be more stable than FDI.
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• Foreign Direct Investment (FDI) involves a
long-term investment in a specific enterprise,
often with management control.
• Foreign Institutional Investment (FII) involves
the purchase of financial assets like stocks
and bonds and is generally short-term.

•

•
•
•
•

Statement (b) is the best description. FII
increases overall capital availability in the
financial markets, while FDI targets a specific
company or sector.
(a) is incorrect because FDI brings
management skills and technology.
(c) is incorrect because FIIs invest in the
secondary market.
(d) is incorrect because FDI is considered
more stable than FII.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q21. India is home to lakhs of person with
disabilities. What are the benefits available to them
under the law?
1. Free schooling till the age of 18 years in
government run schools.
2. Preferential allotment of land for setting up
business.
3. Ramps in public buildings.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• The Rights of Persons with Disabilities Act,
2016 provides a comprehensive legal
framework to protect their rights.
• 1 is correct. The Act mandates free education
for every child with a benchmark disability
between the age of 6 and 18 years.
• 2 is correct. The Act provides for preferential
allotment of land at concessional rates for
various purposes, including setting up a
business.
• 3 is correct. The Act mandates that all public
buildings must be made accessible, which
includes the provision of ramps.
• All three are key provisions under the law.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 2012
Q1. Which of the following would include Foreign
Direct Investment in India?
1. Subsidiaries of foreign companies in India
2. Majority foreign equity holding in Indian
companies
3. Companies exclusively financed by foreign
companies
4. Portfolio investment
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1, 2 , 3 and 4
(b) 2 and 4 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3 only
Answer: (d) 1, 2 and 3 only
Explanation:
• Foreign Direct Investment (FDI) refers to a
long-term investment by a foreign entity in
an enterprise in another country, with the
objective of establishing a lasting interest and
exercising significant influence over
management.
• 1, 2, and 3 are forms of FDI:
o Setting up subsidiaries.
o Acquiring majority equity.
o Establishing a company exclusively
financed by a foreign entity.
• These represent direct investment in
productive assets of the host country.
• 4 is not FDI. Portfolio investment (now called
FPI) involves purchase of financial assets like
stocks/bonds. It is short-term, aimed at
financial returns, and does not involve
management control.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) act as a bankers’
bank. This would imply which of the following?
1. Other banks retain their deposits with the RBI.
2. The RBI lends funds to the commercial banks
in times of need.
3. The RBI advises the commercial banks on
monetary matters.

Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 2 and 3 only
(b) 1 and 2 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (d) 1, 2 and 3
Explanation:
• The RBI’s role as the ‘bankers’ bank’ involves
multiple functions that define its relationship
with commercial banks.
• 1 is correct: Commercial banks must maintain
a percentage of deposits as reserves with RBI
(Cash Reserve Ratio).
• 2 is correct: RBI acts as lender of last resort —
lends to banks during liquidity crunch against
approved securities.
• 3 is correct: As monetary authority, RBI
advises banks on monetary and banking
matters to ensure financial stability.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. Under which of the following circumstances may
‘capital gains‘ arise?
1. When there is an increase in the sales of a
product
2. When there is a natural increase in the value
of the property owned.
3. When you purchase a painting and there is a
growth in its value due to increase in its
popularity.
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 2 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (b) 2 and 3 only
Explanation:
• Capital gains = profit realized from sale of a
capital asset.
• A capital asset is property of any kind held by
a person, whether or not used in business.
• 1 is incorrect: Increase in sales of a product =
revenue/income, not capital gain.

•
•

•

2 is correct: Natural increase in property
value → capital gain when sold.
3 is correct: A painting is a capital asset. Value
increase due to popularity, and subsequent
sale gives capital gain.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q4. Which of the following measures would result in
an increase in the money supply in the economy?
1. Purchase of government securities from the
public by the central bank
2. Deposit of currency in commercial banks by
the public
3. Borrowing by the government from the
central bank
4. Sale of government securities to the public by
the central bank
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 4 only
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (c) 1 and 3
Explanation:
• Increase in money supply = more
liquidity/cash circulating in the economy.
• 1 is correct: Central bank purchases
government securities → injects money into
banking system (OMO) → increases money
supply.
• 2 is incorrect: Public depositing currency =
transfer from people to banks, no net change
in total money.
• 3 is correct: Government borrowing from
central bank → central bank creates new
money (monetizing deficit) → increases
money supply.
• 4 is incorrect: Sale of securities withdraws
money from system.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. Consider the following statement: The price of
any currency in international market is decided by
the

1. World Bank
2. Demand for goods/services provided by the
country concerned
3. Stability of the government of the concerned
country
4. Economic potential of the country in question.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1, 2, 3 and 4
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 3 and 4 only
(d) 1 and 4 only
Answer: (b) 2 and 3 only
Explanation:
• The exchange rate of a currency is
determined by demand and supply forces.
• 1 is incorrect: World Bank is a development
institution, not involved in currency pricing.
• 2 is correct: Demand for goods/services
(exports) → demand for currency → stronger
currency.
• 3 is correct: Government stability boosts
investor confidence, attracts foreign capital,
strengthens currency. Political instability
weakens currency.
• 4 is incorrect: Economic potential affects
currency in long term, not a short-term
determinant of daily price.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. Which of the following can be said to be
essentially the parts of ‘Inclusive Governance’?
1. Permitting the Non-Banking Financial
Companies to do banking
2. Establishing effective District Planning
Committees in all the districts
3. Increasing the government spending on public
health
4. Strengthening the Mid-day Meal Scheme
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3 and 4 only
(c) 2, 3 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (c) 2, 3 and 4 only

Explanation:
• Inclusive Governance = participatory,
transparent, accountable, ensuring
development benefits reach all, especially
marginalized groups.
• 1 is not essential: Allowing NBFCs to do
banking = financial reform, not a core
inclusive governance measure.
• 2, 3, and 4 are key:
o District Planning Committees →
decentralized, participatory planning.
o Public health spending → better
healthcare access.
o Mid-day Meal Scheme → nutrition +
education for children.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. The basic aim of Lead Bank Scheme is that
(a) Big banks should try to open offices in each district
(b) there should be stiff competition among the
various nationalized banks
(c) individual banks should adopt particular districts
for intensive development
(d) all the banks should make intensive efforts to
mobilize deposits
Answer: (c) individual banks should adopt particular
districts for intensive development
Explanation:
• Lead Bank Scheme was introduced in 1969
(Gadgil Study Group).
• Core idea: Assign a lead role to one bank per
district to coordinate credit institutions.
• Aim: Lead banks “adopt” districts, survey
credit needs, expand branches, and ensure
coordinated lending for agriculture & priority
sectors.
• Goal: Intensive economic development of
districts.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q8. Consider the following specific stages of
demographic transition associated with economic
development:
1. Low birth rate with low death rate
2. High birth rate with high death rate

3. High birth rate with low death rate
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1, 2, 3
(b) 2, 1, 3
(c) 2, 3, 1
(d) 3, 2, 1
Answer: (c) 2, 3, 1
Explanation:
• Demographic Transition Model explains
population changes as countries develop.
• Stage 1 (2): High birth & death rates → preindustrial agrarian societies.
• Stage 2 (3): High birth, falling death rates →
health & sanitation improve, population
explosion.
• Stage 3 (1): Low birth & death rates →
urbanization, education, development,
population stabilizes.
• Correct sequence: 2 → 3 → 1.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Cement, Fertilizers, Natural Gas, Refinery

Hence, option (c) is correct.

UPSC PYQs – 2013

Q1. The balance of payments of a country is a
systematic record of
(a) all import and export transactions of a country
during a given period of time, normally a year
(b) goods exported from a country during a year
(c) economic transaction between the government of
one country to another
(d) capital movements from one country to another
Answer: (a)
• The Balance of Payments (BoP) is a
comprehensive and systematic record of all
economic transactions between the residents
of a country and the rest of the world over a
specific period, typically a year.
• It includes not just the import and export of
goods, but also the exchange of services,
capital, and transfer payments. It provides a
complete picture of a country's international
economic position.
• The BoP is divided into two main accounts:
the Current Account (which records trade in
goods and services, investment income, and
unilateral transfers) and the Capital Account
(which records flows of capital).
Hence, option (a) is the Correct Answer.

Q2. The Reserve Bank of India regulates the
commercial banks in matters of
1. liquidity of assets

2. branch expansion
3. merger of banks
4. winding-up of banks
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below.
(a) 1 and 4 only
(b) 2, 3 and 4 only
(c) 1, 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (d)
• The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) is the central
bank and the primary regulator of the
banking system in India. It has extensive
powers under the Banking Regulation Act,
1949, and the RBI Act, 1934, to regulate and
supervise commercial banks.
• 1. Liquidity of assets: The RBI mandates that
banks maintain a certain portion of their
deposits as liquid assets, through the Cash
Reserve Ratio (CRR) and the Statutory
Liquidity Ratio (SLR).
• 2. Branch expansion: Banks require prior
approval from the RBI to open new branches
or close existing ones.
• 3. Merger of banks: The RBI's approval is
mandatory for any merger or amalgamation
of banking companies.
• 4. Winding-up of banks: The RBI can initiate
the process of liquidation or winding-up of a
bank if it is deemed necessary to protect the
interests of depositors.
All four are key regulatory functions of the RBI.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. An increase in the Bank Rate generally indicates
that the
(a) market rate of interest is likely to fall
(b) Central Bank is no longer making loans to
commercial banks
(c) Central Bank is following an easy money policy
(d) Central Bank is following a tight money policy
Answer: (d)
• The Bank Rate is one of the policy rates used
by a central bank to regulate the money

supply. It is the rate at which the central
bank lends money to commercial banks.
• An increase in the Bank Rate makes it more
expensive for commercial banks to borrow
from the central bank. This, in turn, leads
commercial banks to increase their own
lending rates.
• Higher interest rates discourage borrowing
and spending by businesses and consumers,
which helps to reduce the money supply and
control inflation. This is known as a tight
money policy or a contractionary monetary
policy.
• Conversely, a decrease in the Bank Rate
signals an easy money policy.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4. In India, deficit financing is used for raising
resources for
(a) economic development
(b) redemption of public debt
(c) adjusting the balance of payments
(d) reducing the foreign debt
Answer: (a)
• Deficit financing refers to the practice of
financing a government's budget deficit (the
gap between its expenditure and revenue) by
borrowing from the central bank, which
essentially involves the creation of new
money.
• In the context of India, particularly during the
planning era, deficit financing was used as a
key tool to raise resources for public
investment and economic development.
• The government would resort to this method
to fund its large-scale development projects
under the Five-Year Plans when resources
from taxation and public borrowing were
insufficient. While it has inflationary risks, it
was seen as a necessary tool to kick-start
investment and growth.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

bonds. It is a form of capital flow and is
recorded in the Capital Account.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q5. Which of the following constitute Capital
Account?
1. Foreign Loans
2. Foreign Direct Investment
3. Private Remittances
4. Portfolio Investment
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below.
(a) 1, 2 and 3
(b) 1, 2 and 4
(c) 2, 3 and 4
(d) 1, 3 and 4
Answer: (b)
• The Capital Account of the Balance of
Payments records all international
transactions that involve a change in the
ownership of assets. It essentially tracks the
flow of capital into and out of a country.
• 1. Foreign Loans: These are borrowings from
other countries and represent a capital
inflow. They are a key component of the
capital account.
• 2. Foreign Direct Investment (FDI): This is a
long-term investment by a foreign entity in
an enterprise in another country. It is a major
component of the capital account.
• 3. Private Remittances: These are transfers of
money by foreign workers to their home
country. Remittances are considered
unilateral transfers and are recorded in the
Current Account, not the Capital Account.
• 4. Portfolio Investment: This involves the
purchase of financial assets like stocks and

Q6. Consider the following statements:
1. Inflation benefits the debtors.
2. Inflation benefits the bond-holders.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (a)
• Inflation is a sustained increase in the general
price level of goods and services in an
economy over a period of time. When the
price level rises, the purchasing power of
money falls.
• 1 is correct. Inflation benefits debtors. This is
because debtors repay their loans with money
that is worth less than the money they
originally borrowed. The real value of their
debt decreases.
• 2 is incorrect. Inflation harms bond-holders
(creditors). A bond typically pays a fixed
interest rate. During inflation, the real return
on the bond (the nominal interest rate minus
the inflation rate) decreases. The fixed
payments they receive from the bond can buy
fewer goods and services.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q7. Disguised unemployment generally means
(a) large number of people remain unemployed
(b) alternative employment is not available
(c) marginal productivity of labour is zero
(d) productivity of workers is low
Answer: (c)
• Disguised unemployment, also known as
hidden unemployment, is a situation where
more people are employed in a job than are
actually required.
• The key characteristic of disguised
unemployment is that the marginal
productivity of the surplus labour is zero. This

means that if the extra workers were
removed, the total output would not
decrease.
• This phenomenon is common in the
agricultural sector of developing countries,
where family members may all work on a
small farm, but the work could be done by
fewer people without any loss of production.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q8. Consider the following liquid assets:
1. Demand deposits with the banks
2. Time deposits with the banks
3. Savings deposits with the banks
4. Currency
The correct sequence of these in decreasing order of
Liquidity is
(a) 1-4-3-2
(b) 4-3-2-1
(c) 2-3-1-4
(d) 4-1-3-2
Answer: (d)
• Liquidity refers to the ease with which an
asset can be converted into cash without
affecting its market price.
• 4. Currency (cash) is the most liquid asset as
it is the medium of exchange itself.
• 1. Demand deposits (current accounts) are
highly liquid as they can be withdrawn on
demand through checks or ATMs.
• 3. Savings deposits are also highly liquid, but
they may have some minor restrictions on
withdrawal compared to demand deposits.
• 2. Time deposits (fixed deposits) are the least
liquid among the given options. They are held
for a fixed period, and premature withdrawal
often incurs a penalty.
• Therefore, the correct sequence in decreasing
order of liquidity is: Currency > Demand
deposits > Savings deposits > Time deposits.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q9. In the context of Indian economy, Open Market
Operations’ refers to
(a) borrowing by scheduled banks from the RBI

(b) lending by commercial banks to industry and trade
(c) purchase and sale of government securities by the
RBI
(d) None of the above
Answer: (c)
• Open Market Operations (OMOs) are a key
monetary policy tool used by the Reserve
Bank of India (RBI).
• They involve the purchase and sale of
government securities (G-Secs) in the open
market to regulate the liquidity in the
banking system.
• When the RBI wants to increase liquidity, it
buys government securities from the market,
injecting money into the system.
• When it wants to reduce liquidity, it sells
government securities, absorbing money
from the system.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q10. Priority Sector Lending by banks in India
constitutes the lending to
(a) Agriculture
(b) Micro and small enterprises
(c) Weaker sections
(d) All of the above
Answer: (d)
• Priority Sector Lending (PSL) is a mandate
from the RBI to banks, requiring them to
devote a certain percentage of their lending
to specific sectors of the economy that are
considered important for the country's
development but may not get timely and
adequate credit otherwise.
• The priority sector includes:
o Agriculture
o Micro, Small and Medium Enterprises
(MSMEs)
o Export Credit
o Education
o Housing
o Social Infrastructure
o Renewable Energy
o Loans to Weaker Sections (which
includes small and marginal farmers,

artisans, beneficiaries of
government-sponsored schemes,
etc.)
• Therefore, all the options listed are part of the
priority sector.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q11. To obtain full benefits of demographic
dividend, what should India do?
(a) Promoting skill development
(b) Introducing more social security schemes
(c) Reducing infant mortality rate
(d) Privatization of higher education
Answer: (a)
• Demographic dividend refers to the
economic growth potential that can result
from having a large share of the population in
the working-age group (15–64 years).
• To realize this dividend, the large working-age
population must be educated, healthy, and
productively employed.
• Promoting skill development is the most
crucial step to ensure that the young
workforce has the necessary skills to be
employable and contribute to economic
growth. A skilled workforce can drive
innovation, increase productivity, and attract
investment.
• While social security, reducing infant
mortality, and education are all important,
skill development directly addresses the
challenge of making the demographic
dividend a reality by turning the young
population into a productive asset.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q12. A rise in general level of prices may be caused
by
1. an increase in the money supply
2. a decrease in the aggregate level of output
3. an increase in the effective demand
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2 only

(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (d)
• A rise in the general price level is known as
inflation. It can be caused by factors that
increase demand (demand-pull inflation) or
factors that reduce supply (cost-push
inflation).
• 1. An increase in the money supply: When
there is more money chasing the same
amount of goods and services, it leads to an
increase in prices. This is a classic cause of
demand-pull inflation.
• 2. A decrease in the aggregate level of
output: When the overall production of
goods and services in the economy falls (due
to factors like crop failure, supply chain
disruptions, etc.), but demand remains the
same, there are fewer goods available,
leading to a rise in prices (cost-push
inflation).
• 3. An increase in the effective demand: When
the total demand for goods and services in
the economy (from consumers, businesses,
and the government) rises faster than the
economy's ability to produce them, it pulls
prices up.
All three factors can contribute to a rise in the general
price level.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q13. Which one of the following groups of items is
included in India’s foreign-exchange reserves?
(a) Foreign-currency assets, Special Drawing Rights
(SDRs) and loans from foreign countries
(b) Foreign-currency assets, gold holdings of the RBI
and SDRs
(c) Foreign-currency assets, loans from the World
Bank and SDRs
(d) Foreign-currency assets, gold holdings of the RBI
and loans from the World Bank
Answer: (b)
• India's foreign-exchange reserves (forex
reserves) are assets held by the Reserve Bank
of India (RBI) in foreign currencies. They are

used to back liabilities and influence
monetary policy.
• The components of India's forex reserves are:
1. Foreign Currency Assets (FCA): These
are the largest component and
include assets like US dollars, Euros,
Pound sterling, Japanese yen, etc.,
held in the form of foreign
government securities.
2. Gold Holdings of the RBI: The RBI
holds a significant amount of gold as
part of its reserves.
3. Special Drawing Rights (SDRs): This is
an international reserve asset
created by the IMF, and its value is
based on a basket of five major
currencies.
4. Reserve Tranche Position (RTP) in the
IMF: This represents a portion of the
required quota of currency each
member country must provide to the
IMF that can be readily drawn upon.
• Loans from foreign countries or the World
Bank are liabilities, not reserve assets.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q14. Which one of the following is likely to be the
most inflationary in its effect?
(a) Repayment of public debt
(b) Borrowing from the public to finance a budget
deficit
(c) Borrowing from banks to finance a budget deficit
(d) Creating new money to finance a budget deficit
Answer: (d)
• Inflation is caused by an increase in the
money supply without a corresponding
increase in the output of goods and services.
• (a) Repayment of public debt: This involves
the government paying back money to the
public, which can increase liquidity but is
generally not as inflationary as creating new
money.
• (b) Borrowing from the public: When the
government borrows from the public, it is
essentially transferring existing money from

the public to the government. It does not
create new money and has a minimal
inflationary impact.
• (c) Borrowing from banks: This can be
inflationary as it increases the credit creation
capacity of banks, but it is still based on
existing deposits to some extent.
• (d) Creating new money to finance a budget
deficit (deficit financing or monetization of
the deficit): This involves the government
borrowing from the central bank, which then
prints new money to cover the deficit. This
directly injects new, high-powered money
into the economy without any corresponding
increase in production, making it the most
inflationary method of financing a deficit.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q15. Supply of money remaining the same when
there is an increase in demand for money, there will
be
(a) a fall in the level of prices
(b) an increase in the rate of interest
(c) a decrease in the rate of interest
(d) an increase in the level of income and employment
Answer: (b)
• In the money market, the rate of interest is
the price of money. It is determined by the
interaction of the demand for money and the
supply of money.
• The supply of money is assumed to be fixed
by the central bank in the short run.
• If the demand for money increases (people
want to hold more cash for transactions,
precautionary, or speculative motives) while
the supply of money remains the same, there
will be a shortage of money.
• To encourage people to hold less money and
lend more, the price of money, which is the
rate of interest, must rise. A higher interest
rate is the reward for parting with liquidity.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
