UPSC PYQs – 2014
Q1. In the context of food and nutritional security of
India, enhancing the ‘Seed Replacement Rates’ of
various crops helps in achieving the food production
targets of the future. But what is/are the

constraint/constraints in its wider / greater
implementation?
1. There is no National Seeds Policy in place.
2. There is no participation of private sector
seed companies in the supply of quality seeds
of vegetables and planting materials of
horticultural crops.
3. There is a demand-supply gap regarding
quality seeds in case of low value and high
volume crops.
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 3 only
(c) 2 and 3
(d) None
Answer: (b)
• Seed Replacement Rate (SRR) is the
percentage of area sown out of total area of
crop planted in the season by using
certified/quality seeds other than the farm
saved seed.
• 1 is incorrect. India has a National Seeds
Policy, which was formulated in 2002. This
policy aims to provide farmers with a wide
range of high-quality seeds and to protect the
interests of farmers and the seed industry.
• 2 is incorrect. The private sector plays a
significant role in the Indian seed industry,
especially in the supply of quality seeds for
high-value crops like vegetables, fruits, and
flowers. Their participation is quite active.
• 3 is correct. There is a significant demandsupply gap for quality seeds, particularly for
low-value and high-volume crops like wheat
and paddy. Private companies often focus on
low-volume, high-value hybrid seeds, while
the public sector struggles to meet the
massive demand for quality seeds of staple
food crops. This gap is a major constraint in
enhancing the overall Seed Replacement
Rate.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. With reference to Union Budget, which of the
following, is/are covered under Non-Plan
Expenditure?
1. Defence expenditure
2. Interest payments
3. Salaries and pensions
4. Subsidies
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1, 2, 3 and 4
(d) None
Answer: (c)
• Non-Plan Expenditure (a classification used
until the 2016–17 budget) referred to all
government expenditure not included in the
Five-Year Plans. It represented the
government's committed or obligatory
spending.
• The major components of Non-Plan
Expenditure included:
o 1. Defence expenditure: Spending on
the armed forces and defence
equipment.
o 2. Interest payments: Payments on
the government's domestic and
external debt.
o 3. Salaries and pensions: Payments to
government employees and retired
personnel.
o 4. Subsidies: Financial support
provided for food, fertilizers, fuel,
etc.
• All the items listed were major components of
the government's Non-Plan Expenditure.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. What are the benefits of implementing the
‘Integrated Watershed Development Programme’?
1. Prevention of soil runoff
2. Linking the country’s perennial rivers with
seasonal rivers
3. Rainwater harvesting and recharge of
groundwater table
4. Regeneration of natural vegetation

Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2, 3 and 4 only
(c) 1, 3 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (c)
• The Integrated Watershed Management
Programme (IWMP) aims at the holistic
development of rainfed and degraded areas
by treating the watershed as a single natural
unit.
• The key objectives and benefits of this
programme are:
o 1. Prevention of soil runoff: By
implementing measures like contour
bunding, terracing, and afforestation,
the programme helps in conserving
soil and water.
o 3. Rainwater harvesting and recharge
of groundwater table: It focuses on
constructing structures like check
dams, percolation tanks, and farm
ponds to harvest rainwater and
augment the groundwater level.
o 4. Regeneration of natural
vegetation: The programme
promotes afforestation and pasture
development to restore the
ecological balance.
• 2 is incorrect. Linking perennial rivers with
seasonal rivers is a separate national-level
project (Inter-linking of Rivers Project) and is
not an objective or activity under the
watershed development programmes, which
operate on a micro, local scale.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4. What does venture capital mean?
(a) A short-term capital provided to industries
(b) A long-term start-up capital provided to new
entrepreneurs
(c) Funds provided to industries at times of incurring
losses
(d) Funds provided for replacement and renovation of
industries

Answer: (b)
• Venture Capital (VC) is a form of private
equity financing that is provided by venture
capital firms or funds to start-ups, earlystage, and emerging companies that have
been deemed to have high growth potential
or which have demonstrated high growth.
• It is a long-term investment, as venture
capitalists expect a return on their
investment over a period of several years.
• VC firms not only provide capital but also
offer mentorship, strategic guidance, and
access to networks to help the new
entrepreneurs build their businesses. It is a
high-risk, high-return investment.
Hence, option (b) is the most accurate description.
Q5. The sales tax you pay while purchasing a
toothpaste is a
(a) tax imposed by the Central Government
(b) tax imposed by the Central Government but
collected by the State Government
(c) tax imposed by the State Government but
collected by the Central Government
(d) tax imposed and collected by the State
Government
Answer: (d)
• Before the implementation of the Goods and
Services Tax (GST) in 2017, Sales Tax was a
key source of revenue for state governments.
• Sales tax was levied on the sale of goods
within a state. The power to levy this tax was
with the State Governments.
• Therefore, the sales tax paid on a product like
toothpaste was a tax imposed and collected
by the State Government where the purchase
was made.
• The Central Government levied a separate tax
called Central Sales Tax (CST) on inter-state
sales of goods, but the tax on intra-state sales
was a state subject.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q6. With reference to Balance of Payments, which of
the following constitutes/constitute the Current
Account?
1. Balance of trade
2. Foreign assets
3. Balance of invisibles
4. Special Drawing Rights
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 4
Answer: (c)
• The Current Account of the Balance of
Payments records the flow of goods, services,
income, and unilateral transfers between a
country and the rest of the world.
• 1. Balance of trade: This is the difference
between the value of a country's exports and
imports of goods. It is a major component of
the current account.
• 2. Foreign assets: Transactions involving
foreign assets (like FDI, FPI) are recorded in
the Capital Account, not the Current Account.
• 3. Balance of invisibles: This includes the
trade in services (like tourism, software
services), investment income (profits,
dividends, interest), and unilateral transfers
(gifts, grants, remittances). It is a key part of
the current account.
• 4. Special Drawing Rights (SDRs): These are
reserve assets created by the IMF and are
part of the country's foreign exchange
reserves, which are recorded in the Capital
Account.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. The main objective of the 12th Five-Year Plan is
(a) inclusive growth and poverty reductions
(b) inclusive and sustainable growth
(c) sustainable and inclusive growth to reduce
unemployment
(d) faster, sustainable and more inclusive growth.
Answer: (d)

•

The 12th Five-Year Plan of India covered the
period from 2012 to 2017.
• The central theme and main objective of this
plan was “Faster, Sustainable and More
Inclusive Growth.”
• Faster growth referred to accelerating the
rate of economic growth.
• Sustainable growth emphasized the need to
protect the environment and ensure the
long-term viability of the growth process.
• More inclusive growth focused on ensuring
that the benefits of growth are shared by all
sections of society, leading to poverty
reduction, improved health and education,
and reduced inequality.
Hence, option (d) is the correct and complete
objective.
Q8. The terms ‘Marginal Standing Facility Rate’ and
‘Net Demand and Time Liabilities’, sometimes
appearing in news, are used in relation to
(a) banking operations
(b) communication networking
(c) military strategies
(d) supply and demand of agricultural products
Answer: (a)
• Both these terms are fundamental concepts
in the context of banking and monetary
policy in India.
• Marginal Standing Facility (MSF) Rate: This is
a penal rate at which scheduled commercial
banks can borrow money overnight from the
Reserve Bank of India (RBI) when they face
an acute shortage of liquidity. It is a tool for
regulating short-term liquidity.
• Net Demand and Time Liabilities (NDTL): This
represents the total deposit base of a bank. It
is the sum of a bank's demand liabilities (like
current and savings account deposits) and
time liabilities (like fixed deposits), minus the
deposits it holds with other banks. NDTL is the
base on which the RBI calculates the Cash
Reserve Ratio (CRR) and Statutory Liquidity
Ratio (SLR) for banks.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q9. What is/are the facility/facilities the
beneficiaries can get from the services of Business
Correspondent (Bank Saathi) in branchless areas?
1. It enables the beneficiaries to draw their
subsidies and social security benefits in their
villages.
2. It enables the beneficiaries in the rural areas
to make deposits and withdrawals.
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (c)
• Business Correspondents (BCs), also known
as Bank Saathis, are retail agents engaged by
banks to provide banking services in
unbanked and underbanked areas. They are
a key pillar of India's financial inclusion
strategy.
• 1 is correct. BCs play a crucial role in the
Direct Benefit Transfer (DBT) scheme. They
enable beneficiaries to withdraw their
subsidies and social security payments (like
pensions) conveniently in their own villages,
using biometric authentication.
• 2 is correct. The primary function of a BC is to
provide basic banking services. This includes
opening accounts, accepting deposits,
allowing cash withdrawals, and facilitating
remittances.
• Both statements accurately describe the
services provided by Business
Correspondents.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q10. In the context of Indian economy; which of the
following is/are the purpose/purposes of ‘Statutory
Reserve Requirements’?
1. To enable the Central Bank to control the
amount of advances the banks can create
2. To make the people’s deposits with banks
safe and liquid
3. To prevent the commercial banks from
making excessive profits

4. To force the banks to have sufficient vault
cash to meet their day-to-day requirements
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (b)
• Statutory Reserve Requirements refer to the
portion of deposits that commercial banks
are required to hold as reserves rather than
lend out. In India, these requirements consist
of the Cash Reserve Ratio (CRR) and the
Statutory Liquidity Ratio (SLR).
• 1 is correct. By adjusting the reserve
requirements, the Central Bank (RBI) can
control the credit-creating capacity of
commercial banks. An increase in reserve
requirements reduces the funds available for
lending, thus controlling the amount of
advances banks can create. This is a key tool
of monetary policy.
• 2 is correct. These reserves act as a safety
cushion, ensuring that banks have sufficient
liquid assets to meet the withdrawal
demands of their depositors. This makes the
deposits safe and liquid and helps maintain
public confidence in the banking system.
• 3 is incorrect. The purpose of reserve
requirements is not to control bank profits,
although they can indirectly affect profitability
by limiting the amount of funds available for
lending.
• 4 is incorrect. While banks need to manage
their vault cash for day-to-day needs, the
statutory reserve requirements (CRR, which is
kept with the RBI, and SLR, which is held in
specified liquid assets) are distinct from the
bank's own vault cash management.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q11. Consider the following pairs:
Programme/Project — Ministry
1. Drought Prone Area Programme: Ministry of
Agriculture

2. Desert Development Programme: Ministry of
Environment and Forests
3. National Watershed Development Project for
Rainfed Areas: Ministry of Rural Development
Which of the above pairs is/are correctly matched?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3 only
(c) 1, 2 and 3
(d) None
Answer: (d)
• 1 is incorrect. The Drought Prone Area
Programme (DPAP) is implemented by the
Ministry of Rural Development, not the
Ministry of Agriculture. It aims to tackle the
special problems of drought-prone areas.
• 2 is incorrect. The Desert Development
Programme (DDP) is also implemented by the
Ministry of Rural Development, not the
Ministry of Environment and Forests. It
focuses on controlling desertification and
mitigating the adverse effects of drought.
• 3 is incorrect. The National Watershed
Development Project for Rainfed Areas
(NWDPRA) was launched by the Ministry of
Agriculture, not the Ministry of Rural
Development. It focuses on sustainable
development of rainfed areas.
• All three pairs are incorrectly matched.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q12. If the interest rate is decreased in an economy,
it will
(a) decrease the consumption expenditure in the
economy
(b) increase the tax collection of the Government
(c) increase the investment expenditure in the
economy
(d) increase the total savings in the economy
Answer: (c)
• A decrease in the interest rate makes it
cheaper for businesses to borrow money to
finance their projects.
• This lower cost of borrowing incentivizes
firms to undertake new investment projects
(e.g., building new factories, buying new

machinery) that might not have been
profitable at a higher interest rate.
• Therefore, a decrease in the interest rate
generally leads to an increase in investment
expenditure in the economy.
• (a) is incorrect because lower interest rates
usually encourage, not decrease,
consumption.
• (b) is an indirect effect and not a necessary
consequence.
• (d) is incorrect because lower interest rates
can discourage savings as the return on
savings is lower.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q13. Economic growth is usually coupled with
(a) deflation
(b) inflation
(c) stagflation
(d) disinflation
Answer: (b)
• Economic growth generally leads to increased
aggregate demand for goods and services.
• As income and output increase, consumption
and investment typically rise, putting upward
pressure on prices.
• This results in inflation, which is a sustained
rise in the general price level.
• (a) Deflation is a fall in the general price level,
which usually occurs during recessions, not
during growth phases.
• (c) Stagflation refers to the coexistence of
high inflation and low economic growth,
usually accompanied by high unemployment.
• (d) Disinflation is a fall in the inflation rate,
not a fall in prices, and is not a typical feature
of periods of strong economic growth.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q14. Which one of the following is likely to be the
most inflationary in its effect?
(a) Repayment of public debt
(b) Borrowing from the public to finance a budget
deficit

(c) Borrowing from banks to finance a budget deficit
(d) Creating new money to finance a budget deficit
Answer: (d)
• Inflation is caused by an increase in the
money supply without a corresponding
increase in the output of goods and services.
• (a) Repayment of public debt: This involves
the government paying back money to the
public, which can increase liquidity but is
generally not as inflationary as creating new
money.
• (b) Borrowing from the public: When the
government borrows from the public, it is
essentially transferring existing money from
the public to the government. It does not
create new money and has a minimal
inflationary impact.
• (c) Borrowing from banks: This can be
inflationary as it increases the credit creation
capacity of banks, but it is still based on
existing deposits to some extent.
• (d) Creating new money to finance a budget
deficit (deficit financing or monetization of
the deficit): This involves the government
borrowing from the central bank, which then
prints new money to cover the deficit. This
directly injects new, high-powered money
into the economy without any corresponding
increase in production, making it the most
inflationary method of financing a deficit.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q15. A rise in general level of prices may be caused
by
1. an increase in the money supply
2. a decrease in the aggregate level of output
3. an increase in the effective demand
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (d)

•

A rise in the general price level is known as
inflation. It can be caused by factors that
increase demand (demand-pull inflation) or
factors that reduce supply (cost-push
inflation).
• 1. An increase in the money supply: When
there is more money chasing the same
amount of goods and services, it leads to an
increase in prices. This is a classic cause of
demand-pull inflation.
• 2. A decrease in the aggregate level of
output: When the overall production of
goods and services in the economy falls (due
to factors like crop failure, supply chain
disruptions, etc.), but demand remains the
same, there are fewer goods available,
leading to a rise in prices (cost-push
inflation).
• 3. An increase in the effective demand: When
the total demand for goods and services in
the economy (from consumers, businesses,
and the government) rises faster than the
economy's ability to produce them, it pulls
prices up.
All three factors can contribute to a rise in the general
price level.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q16. In India, in the overall Index of Industrial
Production, the indices of eight core industries have
a combined weight of
(a) 37.90%
(b) 38.90%
(c) 39.90%
(d) 40.90%
Answer: (c)
• The Index of Industrial Production (IIP) is a
composite indicator that measures the shortterm changes in the volume of production of
a basket of industrial products.
• The Eight Core Industries — coal, crude oil,
natural gas, refinery products, fertilizers,
steel, cement, and electricity — are
considered the most important sectors of the
economy.

•

Their combined weight in the IIP is 39.90%.
• These industries have a significant impact on
the overall industrial performance, and
changes in their production strongly influence
the IIP.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q17. Which one of the following is not the most
likely measure the government/RBI takes to stop the
slide of Indian rupee?
(a) Curbing imports of non-essential goods and
promoting exports
(b) Encouraging Indian borrowers to issue rupee
denominated Masala Bonds
(c) Easing conditions relating to external commercial
borrowing
(d) Following an expansionary monetary policy
Answer: (d)
• When the rupee slides (depreciates), the
government and the RBI usually take
measures to stabilize the currency.
• (a) Curbing imports of non-essential goods
and promoting exports helps reduce the
demand for foreign exchange and increase
foreign currency inflows, supporting the
rupee.
• (b) Encouraging Masala Bonds (rupeedenominated bonds) helps raise foreign
capital without increasing foreign exchange
liabilities, thus supporting the rupee.
• (c) Easing external commercial borrowing
conditions encourages more capital inflows,
increasing dollar supply and supporting the
rupee.
• (d) Following an expansionary monetary
policy (e.g., lowering interest rates, increasing
liquidity) would likely worsen the slide
because it can reduce capital inflows and
increase inflationary pressures. This is not a
measure to support the rupee.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q18. With reference to Indian economy, consider the
following:
1. Bank rate

2. Open Market Operations
3. Public debt
4. Public revenue
Which of the above is/are component/components of
Monetary Policy?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2, 3 and 4
(c) 1 and 2
(d) 1, 3 and 4
Answer: (c)
• Monetary Policy refers to the policy
measures of the central bank (RBI in India's
case) that influence the money supply, credit
availability, and interest rates.
• 1. Bank rate: It is a key monetary policy tool
used by the RBI to regulate credit and money
supply.
• 2. Open Market Operations (OMOs): These
involve the purchase and sale of government
securities in the open market by the RBI to
control liquidity in the system.
• 3. Public debt and 4. Public revenue are part
of fiscal policy, which deals with government
income and expenditure, not monetary
policy.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs – 2015
Q1. In India the steel production industry requires
the import of
(a) saltpeter
(b) rock phosphate
(c) coking coal
(d) All of the above
Answer: (c)
• India has abundant reserves of high-quality
iron ore, a primary raw material for steel
production.
• However, the country faces a significant
shortage of high-quality coking coal (also
known as metallurgical coal), which is
essential for the production of steel in blast
furnaces.

•

The domestic coking coal that is available is
often of high ash content and not suitable for
efficient steelmaking. Consequently, the
Indian steel industry is heavily dependent on
imports to meet its coking coal requirements.
• Saltpeter and rock phosphate are primarily
used in the fertilizer industry, not in steel
production.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. Consider the following statements :
1. The Accelerated Irrigation Benefits
Programme was launched during 1996-97 to
provide loan assistance to poor farmers.
2. The Command Area Development Programme
was launched in 1974-75 for the development
of water-use efficiency.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (b)
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The Accelerated
Irrigation Benefits Programme (AIBP) was
launched in 1996–97 to provide financial
assistance to States to expedite the
completion of ongoing major and medium
irrigation projects, not to provide direct loan
assistance to poor farmers.
• Statement 2 is correct. The Command Area
Development (CAD) Programme was
launched in 1974–75 with the primary
objective of improving the utilization of
created irrigation potential and optimizing
agricultural productivity. This was to be
achieved by improving water-use efficiency
through measures like the construction of
field channels.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3. ‘Basel III Accord’ or simply ‘Basel III’, often seen
in the news, seeks to
(a) develop national strategies for the conservation
and sustainable use of biological diversity

(b) improve banking sector’s ability to deal with
financial and economic stress and improve risk
management
(c) reduce the greenhouse gas emissions but places a
heavier burden on developed countries
(d) transfer technology from developed Countries to
poor countries to enable them to replace the use of
chlorofluorocarbons in refrigeration with harmless
chemicals
Answer: (b)
• The Basel Accords (Basel I, II, and III) are a
series of international banking regulations
set by the Basel Committee on Banking
Supervision (BCBS).
• Basel III is a comprehensive set of reform
measures developed in response to the global
financial crisis of 2008. Its primary aim is to
strengthen the regulation, supervision, and
risk management of the banking sector
globally.
• It seeks to improve the banking sector's
ability to absorb shocks arising from financial
and economic stress, thereby reducing the
risk of spillover from the financial sector to
the real economy. Key measures include
stricter capital and liquidity requirements for
banks.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. With reference to India economy, consider the
following statements :
1. The rate of growth of real Gross Domestic
Product has steadily increased in the last
decade.
2. The Gross Domestic Product at market prices
(in rupees) has steadily increased in the last
decade
Which of the statement given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (b)
• This question refers to the decade from
roughly 2005 to 2015.

•

Statement 1 is incorrect. The rate of growth
of real GDP has not increased steadily. It has
fluctuated significantly due to factors like the
2008 global financial crisis, which caused a
slowdown. The growth rate has seen periods
of acceleration and deceleration, not a steady
increase.
• Statement 2 is correct. The GDP at market
prices (nominal GDP) has steadily increased
over the last decade. Even when the growth
rate slowed down, it remained positive. A
positive growth rate means the absolute size
of the economy continues to increase.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. With reference to India economy, consider the
following :
1. Bank rate
2. Open market operations
3. Public debt
4. Public revenue
Which of the above is/are component/components of
Monetary Policy?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2, 3 and 4
(c) 1 and 2
(d) 1, 3 and 4
Answer: (c)
• Monetary Policy refers to the actions
undertaken by a central bank to manipulate
the money supply and credit conditions to
stimulate or restrain economic activity. It is
managed by the Reserve Bank of India (RBI).
• 1. Bank Rate and 2. Open Market Operations
(OMOs) are classic instruments of monetary
policy. The Bank Rate is the rate at which the
RBI lends to commercial banks, and OMOs
involve buying and selling of government
securities to regulate liquidity.
• 3. Public Debt and 4. Public Revenue are
components of Fiscal Policy, which is
managed by the Government.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q6. With reference to inflation in India, which of the
following statements is correct?
(a) Controlling the inflation in India is the
responsibility of the Government of India only
(b) The Reserve Bank of India has no role in controlling
the inflation
(c) Decreased money circulation helps in controlling
the inflation
(d) Increased money circulation helps in controlling
the inflation
Answer: (c)
• Inflation is a sustained increase in the general
price level, often described as “too much
money chasing too few goods.”
• Controlling inflation is a joint effort of the
Government and the RBI.
• (a) and (b) are incorrect. Both the
Government of India (fiscal policy) and the
Reserve Bank of India (monetary policy) have
crucial roles.
• (c) is correct. A key way to control inflation is
to reduce the amount of money in
circulation. Less money means lower
demand, which helps bring down prices —
the aim of tight monetary policy.
• (d) is incorrect. Increased circulation would
fuel demand, not control inflation.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. The substitution of steel for wooden ploughs in
agricultural production is an example of
(a) labour-augmenting technological progress
(b) capital-augmenting technological progress
(c) capital-reducing technological progress
(d) None of the above
Answer: (b)
• Technological progress can be categorized
based on how it affects factors of production
(labor and capital).
• Replacing a wooden plough with a steel
plough enhances the productivity and
efficiency of capital in agriculture.
• This allows more output with the same or less
labor.

•

Therefore, it is an example of capitalaugmenting technological progress.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q8. Which one of the following best describes the
main objective of ‘Seed Village Concept?
(a) Encouraging the farmers to use their own farm
seeds and discouraging them to buy the seeds from
others
(b) Involving the farmers for training in quality seed
production and thereby to make available quality
seeds to others at appropriate time and affordable
cost
(c) Earmarking some villages exclusively for the
production of certified seeds
(d) Identifying the entrepreneurs in village and
providing them technology and finance to set up seed
companies
Answer: (b)
• The Seed Village Concept is a government
initiative aimed at ensuring the availability of
quality seeds at the local level.
• The main objective is to train a group of
farmers in a village in the techniques of
quality seed production.
• These trained farmers then produce seeds for
their own use and also sell them to other
farmers in their village and neighboring areas.
• This model helps in making high-quality seeds
available locally, at the right time, and at an
affordable cost.
• It promotes self-sufficiency in seed
production at the village level and ensures the
rapid spread of improved crop varieties.
Hence, option (b) is the most accurate description.
Q9. The problem of international liquidity is related
to the non-availability of
(a) goods and services
(b) gold and silver
(c) dollars and other hard currencies
(d) exportable surplus
Answer: (c)
• International liquidity refers to the pool of
assets that are readily available and

acceptable for settling international
transactions and financing balance of
payments deficits.
• These assets must be widely accepted
internationally. Historically, gold was the
primary international reserve asset.
• In the modern global economy, the most
important components of international
liquidity are hard currencies like the US
dollar, Euro, Japanese yen, and British
pound.
• A problem of international liquidity arises
when countries do not have sufficient
reserves of these hard currencies to meet
their international payment obligations.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q10. There has been a persistent deficit budget year
after year. Which of the following actions can be
taken by the government to reduce the deficit?
1. Reducing revenue expenditure
2. Introducing new welfare schemes
3. Rationalizing subsidies
4. Expanding industries
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 and 3 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (a)
• A budget deficit occurs when government
expenditure exceeds its revenue. To reduce
the deficit, the government must either
increase revenue or decrease expenditure.
• 1. Reducing revenue expenditure: This is a
direct way to cut spending. Revenue
expenditure includes non-asset creating
expenses like salaries, interest payments,
and administrative costs.
• 2. Introducing new welfare schemes: This
would increase expenditure, worsening the
deficit.
• 3. Rationalizing subsidies: Subsidies are a
major part of revenue expenditure.

Rationalizing them (making them targeted
and efficient) can lead to significant savings.
• 4. Expanding industries: While this may lead
to long-term revenue gains, it often requires
large short-term investment, which could
increase the deficit initially.
Therefore, the most direct measures are reducing
revenue expenditure and rationalizing subsidies.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q11. With Reference to the Fourteenth Finance
Commission, which of the following statements
is/are correct?
1. It has increased the share of States in the
central divisible pool from 32 percent to 42
percent
2. It has made recommendations concerning
sector-specific grants
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (a)
• The Fourteenth Finance Commission (FFC),
chaired by Dr. Y.V. Reddy, made
recommendations for 2015–2020.
• Statement 1 is correct. It increased the share
of states in the net proceeds of Union tax
revenues from 32% to 42%, a landmark step
toward fiscal federalism.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The FFC moved
away from recommending sector-specific
grants. Instead, it emphasized untied funds to
give states greater autonomy and flexibility
in spending.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q12. Pradhan Mantri Jan Dhan Yojana has been
launched for
(a) providing housing loan to poor people at cheaper
interest rates
(b) Promoting women’s Self Help Groups in backward
areas
(c) promoting financial inclusion in the country

(d) providing financial help to marginalised
communities
Answer: (c)
• Pradhan Mantri Jan Dhan Yojana (PMJDY),
launched in August 2014, is a national
mission for financial inclusion.
• Its primary objective is to ensure access to
financial services — banking/savings and
deposit accounts, remittance, credit,
insurance, and pension — in an affordable
manner to all households.
• The scheme focuses on providing a basic
bank account to every unbanked household,
along with a RuPay debit card and an
overdraft facility.
• While it provides financial help indirectly, its
core objective is financial inclusion.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q13. A decrease in tax to GDP ratio of a country
indicates which of the following?
1. Slowing economic growth rates
2. Less equitable distribution of national income
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (a)
• The tax-to-GDP ratio indicates a country's
ability to generate tax revenue relative to its
economy's size.
• 1 is correct. Slowing economic growth is a
primary reason for a decrease in the ratio.
When economic activity slows, profits and
incomes fall, leading to lower tax collections.
• 2 is incorrect. An inequitable distribution
affects the tax base structurally, but the ratio
decrease is more related to short-to-mediumterm economic cycles, not inequality by itself.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

•

The final approval is given by the Cabinet
Committee on Economic Affairs (CCEA),
chaired by the Prime Minister.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q14. In the Index of Eight Core Industries, which one
of the following is given the highest weight?
(a) Coal Production
(b) Electricity generation
(c) Fertilizer Production
(d) Steel Production
Answer: (b)
• The Index of Eight Core Industries is a key
indicator of industrial performance.
• With the 2004–05 base year, the weights
were:
1. Electricity generation – 10.32%
2. Steel – 6.68%
3. Refinery Products – 5.94%
4. Crude Oil – 5.22%
5. Coal – 4.38%
6. Cement – 2.41%
7. Natural Gas – 1.71%
8. Fertilizer – 1.25%
• Electricity generation had the highest weight
at that time.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q15. The Fair and Remunerative Price of Sugarcane is
approved by the
(a) Cabinet Committee on Economic Affairs
(b) Commission for Agricultural Costs and Prices
(c) Directorate of Marketing and Inspection, Ministry
of Agriculture
(d) Agricultural Produce Marketing Committee
Answer: (a)
• The CACP recommends the Fair and
Remunerative Price (FRP) of sugarcane,
considering factors like cost of production
and recovery rate.

Q16. Which one of the following issues the “Global
Economic Prospects” report periodically?
(a) The Asian Development Bank
(b) The European Bank for Reconstruction and
Development
(c) The US Federal Reserve Bank
(d) The World Bank
Answer: (d)
• The “Global Economic Prospects” (GEP) is a
flagship report of the World Bank.
• Published twice a year, it analyzes global
economic trends, focusing on emerging
markets and developing economies.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q17. When the Reserve Bank of India reduces the
Statutory Liquidity by 50 basis points, which of the
following is likely to happen?
(a) India’s GDP growth rate increases drastically
(b) Foreign Institutional Investors may bring more
capital into our country
(c) Scheduled Commercial Banks may cut their lending
rates
(d) It may drastically reduce the liquidity to the
banking system.
Answer: (c)
• Statutory Liquidity Ratio (SLR) is the
percentage of NDTL that banks must keep in
liquid assets.
• When the RBI reduces SLR, banks have to
hold less in reserve, freeing up funds for
lending.
• This increases credit supply, prompting banks
to cut lending rates to attract borrowers.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q18. In India, markets in agricultural products are
regulated under the
(a) Essential Commodities Act, 1955
(b) Agricultural Produce Market Committee Act

enacted by States
(c) Agricultural Produce (Grading and Marking) Act,
1937
(d) Food Products Order, 1956 and Meat and Food
Products Order, 1973
Answer: (b)
• Agriculture is a State subject.
• Most states have enacted their own
Agricultural Produce Market Committee
(APMC) Acts, creating regulated mandis.
• APMC regulates the sale and purchase of
farm produce to protect farmers and ensure
fair prices.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q19. Which of the following brings out the
‘Consumer Price Index Number for the Industrial
Workers’?
(a) The Reserve Bank of India
(b) The Department of Economic Affairs
(c) The Labour Bureau
(d) The department of Personnel and Training
Answer: (c)
• The Consumer Price Index for Industrial
Workers (CPI-IW) measures changes in retail
prices of a fixed basket of goods and services
consumed by industrial workers.
• It is compiled and released by the Labour
Bureau, under the Ministry of Labour and
Employment.
• It is widely used for wage and DA revisions.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q20. Convertibility of rupee implies :
(a) being able to convert rupee notes into gold
(b) allowing the value of rupee to be fixed by market
forces
(c) freely permitting the conversion of rupee to other
currencies and vice versa
(d) developing an international market for currencies
in India
Answer: (c)
• Currency convertibility means that a
country's currency can be freely exchanged

for foreign currencies at market-determined
exchange rates.
• It implies absence of restrictions on
converting domestic currency to foreign
currency and vice versa.
• India currently has current account
convertibility but partial capital account
convertibility.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs – 2016
Q1. Which of the following is/are the
indicator/indicators used by IFPRI to compute the
Global Hunger Index Report?
1. Undernourishment
2. Child stunting
3. Child mortality
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1, 2 and 3
(d) 1 and 3 only
Answer: (c)
• The Global Hunger Index (GHI) is a tool
designed to comprehensively measure and
track hunger at global, regional, and national
levels.
• At the time this question was asked, the GHI
was calculated by the International Food
Policy Research Institute (IFPRI). It used three
equally weighted indicators:
1. Undernourishment: The proportion
of the population that is
undernourished (insufficient caloric
intake).
2. Child Stunting: The proportion of
children under five who suffer from
stunting (low height for age),
reflecting chronic undernutrition.
3. Child Mortality: The mortality rate of
children under five.
• Note: Since 2016, the GHI formula has been
revised to include a fourth indicator: Child
Wasting (low weight for height). However,

based on the context of the question year,
the three indicators listed were correct.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. The establishment of “Payment Banks’ is being
allowed in India to promote Financial inclusion.
Which of the following statements is/are correct in
this context?
1. Mobile telephone companies and
supermarket chains that are owned and
controlled by residents are eligible to be
promoters of Payment Banks.
2. Payment Banks can issue both credit cards
and debit cards.
3. Payment Banks cannot undertake lending
activities.
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 1 and 3 only
(c) 2 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (b)
• Payments Banks are a new category of banks
licensed by the RBI to further financial
inclusion by providing small savings accounts
and payments/remittance services.
• Statement 1 is correct. The RBI guidelines
allow a wide range of entities to be
promoters of Payments Banks, including nonbank PPI issuers, NBFCs, corporate BCs,
mobile telephone companies, and
supermarket chains, provided they are
owned and controlled by residents.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. Payments Banks can
issue ATM/debit cards, however, they cannot
issue credit cards because they are not
permitted to undertake lending activities.
• Statement 3 is correct. A key restriction on
Payments Banks is that they cannot
undertake any lending activities. Their
primary role is to accept deposits and
facilitate payments and remittances.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q3. There has been a persistent deficit budget year
after year. Which action/actions of the following can
be taken by the Government to reduce the deficit?
1. Reducing revenue expenditure
2. Introducing new welfare schemes
3. Rationalizing subsidies
4. Reducing import duty
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1,2,3 and 4
Answer: (c)
• A budget deficit occurs when government
expenditure exceeds its revenue. To reduce
the deficit, the government must either
increase its revenue or decrease its
expenditure.
• 1. Reducing revenue expenditure: This is a
direct way to cut government spending and is
a key measure to control the deficit.
• 2. Introducing new welfare schemes: This
would increase government expenditure,
thereby worsening the budget deficit.
• 3. Rationalizing subsidies: Subsidies are a
major part of revenue expenditure.
Rationalizing them (targeted and efficient)
can lead to significant savings and help
reduce the deficit.
• 4. Reducing import duty: Import duties are a
source of tax revenue. Reducing them would
decrease government revenue, thus
widening the deficit.
Therefore, the actions that would help reduce the
deficit are reducing revenue expenditure and
rationalizing subsidies.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4. Which one of the following is a purpose of
‘UDAY’, a scheme of the Government?
(a) Providing technical and financial assistance to
start-up entrepreneurs in the field or renewable
source of energy.
(b) Providing electricity to every household in the
country by 2018

(c) Replacing the coal-based power plants with natural
gas, nuclear, solar, wind and tidal power plants over a
period of time.
(d) Providing for financial turnaround and revival of
power distribution companies
Answer: (d)
• UDAY stands for Ujwal DISCOM Assurance
Yojana.
• The scheme was launched by the Government
of India in 2015 with the primary purpose of
achieving the financial turnaround and
revival of Power Distribution Companies
(DISCOMs).
• DISCOMs across the country were facing
severe financial stress due to accumulated
losses and outstanding debts. UDAY was
designed to address this by allowing state
governments to take over a portion of the
DISCOMs' debt, thereby improving their
financial health and operational efficiency.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q5. With reference to ‘IFC Masala Bonds’, sometimes
seen in the news, which of the statements given
below is/are correct?
1. The International Finance Corporation, which
offers these bonds, is an arm of the World
Bank.
2. They are the rupee-denominated bonds and
are a source of debt financing for the public
and private sector.
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 Only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (c)
• Statement 1 is correct. The International
Finance Corporation (IFC) is indeed the
private sector investment arm of the World
Bank Group. Its focus is on promoting private
sector development in developing countries.
• Statement 2 is correct. Masala Bonds are
rupee-denominated bonds issued outside
India to foreign investors. They are a way for

Indian entities (public and private sector) to
raise debt financing from international
markets in their local currency. The currency
risk is borne by the investor, not the issuer.
Both statements are correct.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6. In the context of which of the following do you
sometimes find the terms ‘amber box, blue box and
green box’ in the news?
(a) WTO affairs
(b) SAARC affairs
(c) UNFCCC affairs
(d) India-EU negotiations on FTA
Answer: (a)
• The terms ‘Amber Box’, ‘Blue Box’, and
‘Green Box’ are used in the context of the
World Trade Organization (WTO), specifically
within the Agreement on Agriculture.
• These “boxes” are categories of domestic
subsidies and support given by governments
to their agricultural sector.
• Green Box: Subsidies that are considered
non-distorting or minimally distorting to
trade; permitted without limits (e.g.,
research, environmental protection).
• Amber Box: Subsidies that are considered to
distort trade and production; subject to
reduction commitments (e.g., price support,
input subsidies).
• Blue Box: Subsidies linked to productionlimiting programs; an exception to the
general rule for Amber Box subsidies.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q7. Which of the following is/are included in the
capital budget of the Government of India?
1. Expenditure on acquisition of assets like
roads, buildings, machinery, etc.
2. Loans received from foreign governments.
3. Loans and advances granted to the States
and Union Territories.
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only

(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (d)
• The Capital Budget of the government deals
with capital receipts and capital payments —
transactions that lead to a change in the
government's assets or liabilities.
• 1. Expenditure on acquisition of assets:
Capital expenditure as it creates assets for
the government.
• 2. Loans received from foreign governments:
Capital receipt as it creates a liability for the
government.
• 3. Loans and advances granted to States and
Union Territories: Capital expenditure as it
creates an asset (the loan to be repaid).
All three items are part of the Capital Budget.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q8. Recently, which one of the following currencies
has been proposed to be added to the basket of
IMF’s SDR?
(a) Rouble
(b) Rand
(c) Indian Rupee
(d) Renminbi
Answer: (d)
• The Special Drawing Right (SDR) is an
international reserve asset created by the
International Monetary Fund (IMF). Its value
is based on a basket of major international
currencies.
• In November 2015, the IMF announced its
decision to include the Chinese Renminbi
(Yuan) in the SDR basket. This decision
became effective on October 1, 2016.
• The inclusion of the Renminbi was a
significant milestone, reflecting the
increasing role of China in the global
economy and financial system.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q9. With reference to the International Monetary
and Financial Committee (IMFC), consider the
following statements:

1. IMFC discusses matters of concern affecting
the global economy, and advises the
International Monetary Fund (IMF) on the
direction of its work.
2. The World Bank participates as observer in
IMFC’s meetings.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (c)
• The International Monetary and Financial
Committee (IMFC) is a key advisory body to
the IMF's Board of Governors.
• Statement 1 is correct. The primary role of
the IMFC is to discuss matters of common
concern affecting the global economy and to
advise the IMF on the direction of its work; it
provides strategic guidance to the Fund.
• Statement 2 is correct. The World Bank,
along with other international institutions like
the WTO, OECD, and BIS, participates as an
observer in IMFC meetings, ensuring
coordination and cooperation among major
international financial institutions.
Both statements are correct.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q10. ‘Rashtriya Garima Abhiyaan’ is a national
campaign to
(a) rehabilitate the homeless and destitute persons
and provide them with suitable sources of livelihood
(b) release the sex workers from the practice and
provide them with alternative sources of livelihood
(c) eradicate the practice of manual scavenging and
rehabilitate the manual scavengers
(d) release the bonded labourers free their bondage
and rehabilitate them
Answer: (c)
• Rashtriya Garima Abhiyaan (National
Campaign for Dignity) is a national campaign
led by community-based organizations and
civil society members.

•

Its primary objective is to eradicate the
inhuman practice of manual scavenging (the
manual cleaning, carrying, and disposal of
human excreta) and to rehabilitate the
manual scavengers in alternative, dignified
livelihoods.
• The campaign has been instrumental in
raising awareness and advocating for the
strict implementation of laws prohibiting
manual scavenging.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q11. The term ‘stand up India scheme’ is sometimes
seen in the news in the context of
(a) promoting entrepreneurship among SC/ST and
women entrepreneurs
(b) promoting the financial inclusion of the poor
(c) providing financial assistance to the rural poor for
the construction of houses
(d) promoting the use of renewable energy sources
Answer: (a)
• The Stand-Up India Scheme was launched by
the Government of India in April 2016.
• The main purpose is to promote
entrepreneurship at the grassroots level,
focusing on economic empowerment and job
creation among Scheduled Castes (SC),
Scheduled Tribes (ST), and women
entrepreneurs.
• Under the scheme, bank loans between ₹10
lakh and ₹1 crore are facilitated for new
enterprises in the manufacturing, services, or
trading sector.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q12. With reference to ‘Pradhan Mantri Fasal Bima
Yojana’, consider the following statements:
1. Under this scheme, farmers will have to pay a
uniform premium of two percent for any
crop they cultivate in any season of the year.
2. This scheme covers post-harvest losses
arising out of cyclones and unseasonal rains.
Which of the statements given about is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (b)
• Pradhan Mantri Fasal Bima Yojana (PMFBY)
is the government's flagship crop insurance
scheme.
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The premium rates
are not uniform for all crops and seasons.
There is a uniform premium of 2% for all
Kharif crops and 1.5% for all Rabi crops. For
annual commercial and horticultural crops,
the premium is 5%.
• Statement 2 is correct. A key feature of
PMFBY is the coverage of post-harvest losses.
It provides coverage for a period of up to two
weeks from harvesting for crop losses against
cyclones, cyclonic rains, and unseasonal
rains.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q13. The FAO accords the status of ‘Globally
Important Agricultural Heritage Systems (GIAHS)’ to
traditional agricultural systems. What is the overall
goal of this initiative?
1. To provide modern technology, training in
modern farming methods and financial
support to local communities of identified
GIAHS so as to greatly enhance their
agricultural productivity
2. To identify and safeguard eco-friendly
traditional farm practices and their
associated landscapes, agricultural
biodiversity and knowledge systems of the
local communities

3. To provide Geographical Indication status to
all the varieties of agricultural produce in
such identified GIAHS
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 and 3 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (b)
• The GIAHS initiative was started by the Food
and Agriculture Organization (FAO) of the
UN.
• Statement 2 is the core goal: identify and
safeguard the world's outstanding traditional
agricultural systems, conserving their ecofriendly practices, associated landscapes,
agricultural biodiversity, and traditional
knowledge systems of local communities.
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The goal is not to
replace traditional systems with modern
technology, but to support their dynamic
conservation and promote their viability.
• Statement 3 is incorrect. While some
products may achieve GI status, providing GI
to all varieties is not the overall goal of
GIAHS.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q14. The term ‘Core Banking Solution’ is sometimes
in the news. Which of the following statements best
describes/describe this term?
1. It is a networking of a bank’s branches which
enables customers to operate their accounts
from any branch of the bank on its network
regardless of where they open their accounts.
2. It is an effort to increase RBI’s control over
commercial banks through computerization.
3. It is a detailed procedure by which a bank
with huge non-performing assets is taken
over by another bank.
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3

Answer: (a)
• Core Banking Solution (CBS) is a technology
that has revolutionized the banking industry.
• Statement 1 is the best description: CBS is a
centralized system that networks all
branches of a bank, allowing customers to
access and operate their accounts from any
branch, enabling “anywhere, anytime
banking.”
• Statement 2 is incorrect. While
computerization aids oversight, the primary
purpose of CBS is customer convenience and
operational efficiency, not increasing RBI
control.
• Statement 3 is incorrect. This describes a
bank merger/acquisition process, not CBS.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q15. India’s ranking in the ‘Ease of Doing Business
Index’ is sometimes seen in the news. Which of the
following has declared that ranking?
(a) Organization for Economic Cooperation and
Development (OECD)
(b) World Economic Forum
(c) World Bank
(d) World Trade Organization (WTO)
Answer: (c)
• The ‘Ease of Doing Business’ index was a
flagship annual report published by the
World Bank Group.
• It ranked countries based on how conducive
their regulatory environment was for starting
and operating a local business.
• The report measured parameters like starting
a business, getting credit, paying taxes, and
enforcing contracts.
• Note: The World Bank discontinued the
Doing Business report in September 2021.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q16. ‘Net metering’ is sometimes seen in the news in
the context of promoting the
(a) production and use of solar energy by the
households/consumers
(b) use of piped natural gas in the kitchens of

households
(c) installation of CNG kits in motor- cars
(d) installation of water meters in urban households
Answer: (a)
• Net metering is a billing mechanism used to
promote rooftop solar energy.
• It allows consumers who generate their own
electricity from solar power to feed excess
electricity back into the grid.
• A bi-directional meter records electricity
drawn from the grid and electricity fed into
the grid; the consumer is billed only for the
“net” energy consumed.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q17. What is/are the purpose/purposes of the
‘Marginal Cost of Funds based Lending Rate (MCLR)’
announced by RBI?
1. These guidelines help improve the
transparency in the methodology followed by
banks for determining the interest rates on
advances
2. These guidelines help ensure availability of
bank credit & interest rates which are fair to
the borrowers as well as the banks
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (c)
• The MCLR was introduced by the RBI in 2016
to replace the base rate system for pricing
loans.
• Statement 1 is correct. A key purpose was to
improve transparency via a clear and uniform
methodology for banks to calculate lending
rates based on marginal cost of funds.
• Statement 2 is correct. MCLR was intended to
ensure lending rates are fair to both
borrowers and banks, and to improve
monetary policy transmission, so changes in
the RBI’s policy rates are more quickly
reflected in lending rates.
Both statements are correct.

Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q18. With reference to ‘Initiative for Nutritional
Security through intensive Millets Promotion’, which
of the following statements is/are correct?
1. This initiative aims to demonstrate the
improved production and post-harvest
technologies, and to demonstrate value
addition techniques, in an integrated
manner, with cluster approach.
2. Poor, small, marginal and tribal farmers have
larger stake in this scheme.
3. An important objective of the scheme is to
encourage farmers of commercial crops to
shift to millet cultivation by offering them
free kits of critical inputs of nutrients and
micro-irrigation equipment.
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 2 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (c)
• The INSIMP (Initiative for Nutritional Security
through Intensive Millet Promotion) was
launched to promote cultivation and
consumption of millets, which are highly
nutritious.
• Statement 1 is correct. The scheme uses a
cluster approach to demonstrate a package
of improved production technologies, postharvest processing, and value addition
techniques.
• Statement 2 is correct. Millets are often
grown in rainfed, marginal lands by poor,
small, and tribal farmers; these groups have
the largest stake in improving millet
cultivation and profitability.
• Statement 3 is incorrect. The scheme
provides free kits of critical inputs to
encourage millet cultivation, but its objective
is not to encourage a shift from commercial
crops; rather, it aims to improve productivity
where millets are traditionally grown. Also,

the kits do not typically include microirrigation equipment.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q19. The term ‘Base Erosion and profit shifting’ is
sometimes seen in the news in the context of
(a) mining operation by multinational companies in
resource-rich by backward areas
(b) curbing of the tax evasion by multinational
companies
(c) exploitation of genetic resources of a country by
multinational companies
(d) Lack of consideration of environmental costs in the
planning of development projects
Answer: (b)
• Base Erosion and Profit Shifting (BEPS) refers
to tax avoidance strategies used by
multinational companies (MNCs).
• These strategies exploit gaps and mismatches
in the tax rules of different countries to
artificially shift profits from high-tax
jurisdictions to low- or no-tax jurisdictions,
where they have little or no real economic
activity.
• This erodes the tax base of the high-tax
countries. The OECD/G20 BEPS Project is a
major international initiative aimed at
curbing this form of tax avoidance by MNCs.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

UPSC PYQs – 2017
Q1. Consider the following statements:
1. Tax revenue as a percent of GDP of India has
steadily increased in the last decade.
2. Fiscal deficit as a percent of GDP of India has
steadily increased in the last decade.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (d)
• This question refers to the decade from
roughly 2007 to 2017.
• Statement 1 is incorrect. India's tax-to-GDP
ratio has not increased steadily. It has shown
fluctuations, rising in some years and falling in
others, influenced by economic growth, tax
policy changes, and compliance levels. For
example, it saw a dip after the 2008 financial
crisis.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The fiscal deficit as a
percentage of GDP has also not increased
steadily. It spiked after the 2008 crisis but has
since been on a path of fiscal consolidation,
meaning the government has been actively
trying to reduce it. It has fluctuated, not
shown a steady increase.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. With reference to ‘National Investment and
Infrastructure Fund’, which of the following
statements is/are correct?
1. It is an organ of NITI Aayog.
2. It has a corpus of Rs 4,00,000 crore at
present.
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (d)

•

The National Investment and Infrastructure
Fund (NIIF) is a sovereign wealth fund
established by the Government of India.
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The NIIF is not an
organ of NITI Aayog. It was established as a
Category II Alternative Investment Fund (AIF)
under SEBI regulations and is managed by a
professional investment team. It is a separate
entity under the Ministry of Finance.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The initial target
corpus for the NIIF was ₹40,000 crore, not
₹4,00,000 crore. The government's
contribution was to be 49% of this amount,
with the rest to be raised from domestic and
international investors.
Both statements are incorrect.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. The Global Infrastructure Facility is a/an
(a) ASEAN initiative to upgrade infrastructure in Asia
and financed by credit from the Asian Development
Bank.
(b) World Bank collaboration that facilitates the
preparation and structuring of complex infrastructure
Public-Private Partnerships (PPPs) to enable
mobilization of private sector and institutional
investor capital.
(c) Collaboration among the major banks of the world
working with the OECD and focused on expanding the
set of infrastructure projects that have the potential
to mobilize private investment.
(d) UNCTAD funded initiative that seeks to finance
and facilitate infrastructure development in the world
Answer: (b)
• The Global Infrastructure Facility (GIF) is a
global platform launched by the World Bank
Group.
• Its primary purpose is to facilitate the
preparation and structuring of complex
infrastructure projects, particularly PublicPrivate Partnerships (PPPs), in developing
countries.
• The GIF acts as a collaborative platform,
bringing together multilateral development
banks, governments, and private sector

investors to create a pipeline of bankable
infrastructure projects. The ultimate goal is to
mobilize private sector and institutional
investor capital for infrastructure
development.
Hence, option (b) is the most accurate description.
Q4. Which of the following gives ‘Global Gender Gap
Index’ ranking to the countries of the world?
(a) World Economic Forum
(b) UN Human Rights Council
(c) UN Women
(d) World Health Organization
Answer: (a)
• The Global Gender Gap Report, which
includes the Global Gender Gap Index, is an
annual publication of the World Economic
Forum (WEF).
• The index benchmarks countries on their
progress towards gender parity in four key
dimensions: Economic Participation and
Opportunity, Educational Attainment, Health
and Survival, and Political Empowerment.
• It is one of the most widely cited indices for
measuring gender equality across the globe.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. Which of the following statements is/are correct
regarding the Monetary Policy Committee (MPC)?
1. It decides the RBI’s benchmark interest rates.
2. It is a 12-member body including the
Governor of RBI and is reconstituted every
year.
3. It functions under the chairmanship of the
Union Finance Minister.
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2 only
(c) 3 only
(d) 2 and 3 only
Answer: (a)
• The Monetary Policy Committee (MPC) was
constituted in 2016 to bring more
transparency and accountability to India's
monetary policy.

•

Statement 1 is correct. The primary mandate
of the MPC is to determine the policy interest
rate (repo rate) required to achieve the
inflation target.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The MPC is a 6member body, not 12. It consists of three
members from the RBI (the Governor, a
Deputy Governor, and one officer) and three
external members appointed by the
government. The committee is a permanent
statutory body, not reconstituted every year.
• Statement 3 is incorrect. The MPC functions
under the chairmanship of the Governor of
the RBI, not the Union Finance Minister.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q6. The Trade Disputes Act of 1929 provided for
(a) The participation of workers in the management of
industries.
(b) Arbitrary powers to the management to quell
industrial disputes.
(c) An intervention by the British Court in the event of
a trade dispute.
(d) A system of tribunals and a ban on strikes.
Answer: (d)
• The Trade Disputes Act of 1929 was a key
piece of labor legislation in British India.
• Its main provisions were:
o The establishment of Courts of
Inquiry and Boards of Conciliation to
investigate and settle trade disputes.
This created a formal system of
tribunals.
o It prohibited strikes and lock-outs in
public utility services without prior
notice.
o It declared any strike or lock-out
illegal if its object was not the
furtherance of a trade dispute within
the specific industry and if it was
designed to inflict hardship on the
community to compel the
government. This effectively acted as
a ban on general strikes and political
strikes.

Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q7. The term ‘Digital Single Market Strategy’ seen in
the news refers to
(a) ASEAN
(b) BRICS
(c) EU
(d) G20
Answer: (c)
• The ‘Digital Single Market Strategy’ is a major
policy initiative of the European Union (EU).
• It was launched by the European Commission
in 2015 with the aim of removing barriers to
online trade and services across the EU.
• The strategy seeks to create a single, unified
market for digital goods, services, and
content, allowing for the free movement of
data and ensuring that consumers and
businesses can seamlessly access and engage
in online activities across all EU member
states.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q8. What is the purpose of setting up of Small
Finance Banks (SFBs) in India?
1. To supply credit to small business units
2. To supply credit to small and marginal farmers
3. To encourage young entrepreneurs to set up
business particularly in rural areas.
Select the correct answer from the code given below:
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (a)
• Small Finance Banks (SFBs) are a specific
category of banks created by the RBI to
advance financial inclusion.
• The primary objectives of SFBs are:
o 1. To supply credit to small business
units: This includes micro and small
enterprises.
o 2. To supply credit to small and
marginal farmers: This is a key focus

area to deepen financial services in
the agricultural sector.
o To provide basic banking services
(acceptance of deposits and lending)
to unserved and underserved
sections of the population.
• 3 is incorrect. While SFBs can help
entrepreneurs by providing credit, their
purpose is not specifically to encourage
young entrepreneurs to set up business
particularly in rural areas.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q9. With reference to ‘Asia Pacific Ministerial
Conference on Housing and Urban Development
(APMCHUD)’, consider the following statements:
1. The first APMCHUD was held in India in 2006
on the theme ‘Emerging Urban Forms —

2. India hosts all the Annual Ministerial

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (d)
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The first APMCHUD
was indeed held in New Delhi, India in 2006,
but the theme was ‘A Vision for Sustainable
Urbanization in the Asia-Pacific by 2020’, not
‘Emerging Urban Forms — Policy Responses
and Governance Structure’.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. APMCHUD is a
biennial (held every two years) conference,
not annual. The hosting rotates among
member countries. India does not host all the
conferences.
Both statements are incorrect.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q10. With reference to the ‘National Intellectual
Property Rights Policy’, consider the following
statements:

1. It reiterates India’s commitment to the Doha
Development Agenda and the TRIPS
Agreement.
2. Department of Industrial Policy and
Promotion is the nodal agency for regulating
intellectual property rights in India.
Which of the above statements is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (c)
• The National IPR Policy was approved by the
Indian government in 2016 to create a
comprehensive framework for intellectual
property in the country.
• Statement 1 is correct. The policy explicitly
states that India is committed to its
international obligations, including the Doha
Development Agenda and the TRIPS
Agreement, and will utilize the flexibilities
provided within these agreements to meet its
developmental goals.
• Statement 2 is correct. The policy designated
the Department of Industrial Policy and
Promotion (DIPP) (now Department for
Promotion of Industry and Internal Trade) as
the nodal agency for all IPR-related matters
in India, bringing various IPR offices under
one umbrella for better coordination.
Both statements are correct.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q11. What is/are the advantage/advantages of
implementing the ‘National Agriculture Market’
scheme?
1. It is a pan-India electronic trading portal for
agricultural commodities.
2. It provides the farmers access to nationwide
market, with prices commensurate with the
quality of their produce.
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only

(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (c)
• The National Agriculture Market (NAM), or eNAM, is a major government initiative to
reform agricultural marketing.
• Statement 1 is correct. e-NAM is a pan-India
electronic trading portal that networks the
existing APMC mandis to create a unified
national market for agricultural
commodities.
• Statement 2 is correct. A key advantage is
that it provides farmers with access to a
nationwide market. This allows them to sell
their produce to buyers across the country,
not just in their local mandi. This increased
competition helps them discover better
prices, and the system also facilitates qualitybased pricing.
Both statements are correct.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q12. What is the importance of developing Chabahar
Port by India?
(a) India’s trade with African countries will
enormously increase.
(b) India’s relations with oil-producing Arab countries
will be strengthened.
(c) India will not depend on Pakistan for access to
Afghanistan and Central Asia.
(d) Pakistan will facilitate and protect the installation
of a gas pipeline between Iraq and India.
Answer: (c)
• The development of the Chabahar Port in Iran
is a key strategic project for India.
• Its primary importance lies in providing India
with a direct and reliable sea-land access
route to Afghanistan and the resource-rich
Central Asian republics, bypassing Pakistan.
• Currently, India's land access to these regions
is blocked by Pakistan. The Chabahar Port
provides an alternative route that is crucial
for India's trade, strategic, and geopolitical
interests in the region.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q13. Which of the following has/have occurred in
India after its liberalization of economic policies in
1991?
1. Share of agriculture in GDP increased
enormously.
2. Share of India's exports in world trade
increased.
3. FDI inflows increased.
4. India's foreign exchange reserves increased
enormously.
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1 and 4 only
(b) 2, 3 and 4 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (b)
• The economic liberalization of 1991 led to
significant changes in the Indian economy.
• 1 is incorrect. The share of agriculture in
India's GDP has steadily declined since 1991,
as the services and industrial sectors have
grown much faster.
• 2 is correct. The opening up of the economy
and trade liberalization led to a significant
increase in India's exports and its share in
world trade.
• 3 is correct. The reforms made India a more
attractive destination for foreign investment,
leading to a substantial increase in FDI
inflows.
• 4 is correct. From a near-bankrupt situation
in 1991, India's foreign exchange reserves
have increased enormously, providing a
strong buffer against external shocks.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
