the process of raising capital through the sale
of shares. It is an example of non-debt capital
receipts.
• Capital Expenditure (CapEx) of a Company:
• When a company funds are used to acquire,
upgrade, and maintain physical assets such as
property, plants, buildings, technology, or
equipment are known as Capital Expenditure
(CapEx) of a Company.
• When a company borrows money to be paid
back at a future date with interest it is known
as debt financing. Example: Repayment of
loan is an example of capital…
• Equity financing:
• Equity financing is the process of raising
capital through the sale of shares. It is an
example of non-debt capital receipts.
• Capital receipts are receipts that create
liabilities or reduce financial…
• Hence option correct.
Q6. With reference to Indian economy, consider the
following statements:
1. A share of the household financial savings goes
towards government borrowings.
2. Dated securities issued at market related rates in
auctions form a large component of internal debt.
Which of the above statements is/are correct?

(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct: The household
financial savings used in part to finance
government borrowing. Government
securities (G-secs and T-bills) are issued as a
means of borrowing.
• Statement 2 is correct: Over 93% of the total
state debt is made up of internal debt.
Internal loans, which account for the majority
of the public debt, are further separated into
marketable and non-marketable debt.
• Hence option correct.
Q7. “Rapid Financing Instrument” and “Rapid Credit
Facility” are related to the provisions of lending by
which one of the following?
(a) Asian Development Bank
(b) International Monetary Fund
(c) United Nations Environment Programme Finance
Initiative
(d) World Bank
The correct option is (b).
• Rapid Financing Instrument (RFI) and Rapid
Credit Facility (RCF) are facilities of the
International Monetary Fund (IMF) which
provide financial assistance to countries in
need.
• Rapid Financing Instrument (RFI)
o The RFI provides rapid financial
assistance, available to all member
countries facing urgent balance of
payments needs.
o The RFI replaced the IMF’s previous
emergency assistance policy and is
used in a wide range of
circumstances.
• Rapid Credit Facility (RCF)
o The RCF provides rapid concessional
financial assistance to low-income
countries facing urgent BoP needs

•

with no ex-post conditionality where
a full-fledged program is not feasible.
o The RCF was created under the
Poverty Reduction and Growth Trust
(PRGT) as part of reforms to make
IMF support more flexible.
Hence option correct.

Q8. With reference to the Indian economy, consider
the following statements:
1. An increase in Nominal Effective Exchange Rate
(NEER) indicates the appreciation of rupee.
2. An increase in the Real Effective Exchange Rate
(REER) indicates an improvement in trade
competitiveness.
3. An increasing trend in domestic inflation relative to
inflation in other countries is likely to cause an
increasing divergence between NEER and REER.
Which of the statements are correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct: NEER is the weighted
average of bilateral nominal exchange rates
of the home currency in terms of foreign
currencies. Therefore, an increase in NEER
indicates appreciation of the Rupee against
the weighted basket of currencies.
• Statement 2 is incorrect: REER is the
weighted average of nominal exchange rates
adjusted for relative price differentials. An
increase in REER indicates that exports are

•

•

becoming more expensive and imports
cheaper, i.e., loss of trade competitiveness,
not improvement.
Statement 3 is correct: REER = NEER adjusted
by relative price levels/costs (inflation
differentials). Higher domestic inflation
relative to trading partners will increase the
divergence between NEER and REER.
Hence option correct.

Q9. Consider the following statements:
1. Tight monetary policy of the US Federal Reserve
could lead to capital flight.
2. Capital flight may increase the interest cost of firms
with existing External Commercial Borrowings (ECBs).
3. Devaluation of domestic currency decreases the
currency risk associated with ECBs.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct: A tight monetary
policy (higher interest rates) in the US can
attract global capital back to the US, leading
to capital flight from emerging markets.
• Statement 2 is correct: Capital flight can
reduce liquidity and raise domestic interest
rates, thereby increasing interest costs for
firms with ECBs.
• Statement 3 is incorrect: Devaluation (a fall
in the domestic currency value) increases the
domestic currency cost of servicing foreign
currency ECBs, thereby increasing currency
risk, not decreasing it.
• Hence option correct.
Q10. With reference to the Indian economy, what
are the advantages of “Inflation-Indexed Bonds
(IIBs)”?
1. Government can reduce the coupon rates on its
borrowing by way of IIBs.
2. IIBs provide protection to the investors from
uncertainty regarding inflation.

3. The interest received as well as capital gains on IIBs
are not taxable.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (a).
• Statements 1 and 2 are correct: InflationIndexed Bonds (IIBs) protect savings from
inflation and offer positive real returns. The
principal and interest are linked to WPI/CPI.
They can be traded in the secondary market
and help the Government reduce coupon
rates on its borrowings.
• Statement 3 is incorrect: Existing tax
provisions apply to interest and capital gains
on IIBs; there is no special tax exemption.
• Hence option correct.
Q11. Which of the following activities constitute a
real sector in the economy?
1. Farmers harvesting their crops
2. Textile mills converting raw cotton into fabrics
3. A commercial bank lending money to a trading
company
4. A corporate body issuing Rupee Denominated
Bonds overseas.
Select the correct answer using the code given
below.
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2, 3 and 4 only
(c) 1, 3 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (a).
• The real sector comprises activities that
produce goods and services contributing
directly to GDP.
• Statements 1 and 2 are correct: Farming and
manufacturing (textile mills) are real sector
activities.
• Statements 3 and 4 are incorrect: Bank
lending and issuing bonds overseas are
financial sector activities, not real sector
activities.

•

Hence option correct.

Q12. With reference to Convertible Bonds, consider
the following statements:
1. As there is an option to exchange the bond for
equity, Convertible Bonds pay a lower rate of interest.
2. The option to convert to equity affords the
bondholder a degree of indexation to rising consumer
prices.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct: Convertible bonds are
debt securities that can be converted into
equity; they typically pay a lower coupon
because the conversion option has value.
• Statement 2 is correct: The conversion option
gives bondholders some exposure to equity
upside, which can serve as a partial hedge
against inflation since equity values may rise
with price levels.
• Hence option correct.

UPSC PYQs – 2023
Q1.Consider the following statements:
Statement-I: Interest income from the deposits in
Infrastructure Investment Trusts (InvITs) distributed to
their investors is exempted from tax, but the dividend
is taxable.
Statement-II: InvITs are recognized as borrowers
under the ‘Securitization and Reconstruction of
Financial Assets and Enforcement of Security Interest
Act, 2002’.

Which one of the following is correct in respect of the
above statements?
(a) Both Statement-I and Statement-II are correct and
Statement-II is the correct explanation for Statement-I
(b) Both Statement-I and Statement-II are correct and
Statement-II is not the correct explanation for
Statement-I
(c) Statement-I is correct but Statement-II is incorrect
(d) Statement-I is incorrect but Statement-II is correct
The correct option is (d).
• Infrastructure Investment Trusts (InvITs) are
infrastructure developer-sponsored trusts
that own, operate, and invest in completed
and under-construction infrastructure
projects such as roads, highways, power
distribution networks, telecom towers, and
fiber optic networks.
• Statement I is incorrect: Any dividend or
interest income received from an InvIT is fully
taxable as per the investor’s Income Tax Slab
Rate under the head “Income from Other
Sources”. Capital gains tax applies only when
InvIT units are sold.
• Statement II is correct: InvITs and REITs are
recognized as borrowers under the SARFAESI
Act, 2002, enabling lenders to enforce
security interests. This provides statutory
protection for banks lending to these trusts.
• Hence option correct.
Q2.Consider the following statements:
Statement-I: In the post-pandemic recent past, many
Central Banks worldwide had carried out interest rate
hikes.
Statement-II: Central Banks generally assume that
they have the ability to counteract the rising
consumer prices via monetary policy means.
Which one of the following is correct in respect of the
above statements?
(a) Both Statement-I and Statement-II are correct and
Statement-II is the correct explanation for Statement-I
(b) Both Statement-I and Statement-II are correct and
Statement-II is not the correct explanation for
Statement-I

(c) Statement-I is correct but Statement-II is incorrect
(d) Statement-I is incorrect but Statement-II is correct
The correct option is (a).
• Statement I is correct: In the post-pandemic
period, many central banks increased
interest rates to counter rising inflation and
restore financial stability.
• Statement II is correct: Central banks use
monetary policy tools (interest rate changes,
open market operations, etc.) to manage
inflation and stabilize prices. They assume
such policy adjustments can counteract rising
consumer prices.
• Hence, both statements are correct, and
Statement II explains Statement I.
• Hence option correct.
Q3. Which one of the following activities of the
Reserve Bank of India is considered to be part of
‘sterilization’?
(a) Conducting ‘Open Market Operations’
(b) Oversight of settlement and payment systems
(c) Debt and cash management for the Central and
State Governments
(d) Regulating the functions of Non-banking Financial
Institutions
The correct option is (a).
• Sterilization is a monetary policy operation
used by central banks to neutralize the
effects of foreign capital inflows or outflows
on the domestic money supply.
• It involves buying or selling financial assets
through Open Market Operations (OMOs).
• The RBI uses OMOs to offset the effects of
foreign exchange intervention on liquidity
and to maintain exchange rate stability.
• Hence option correct.
Q4. Consider the following markets:
1. Government Bond Market
2. Call Money Market
3. Treasury Bill Market
4. Stock Market
How many of the above are included in capital
markets?

(a) Only one
(b) Only two
(c) Only three
(d) All four
The correct option is (b).
• Capital Market deals with long-term financial
instruments like stocks and bonds.
o Includes Government Bond Market
o Includes Stock Market
• Money Market deals with short-term
instruments like Call Money and Treasury
Bills (both less than one-year maturity).
o Call Money Market
o Treasury Bill Market
• Hence, only two markets (Government Bond
Market and Stock Market) are part of the
Capital Market.
• Hence option correct.
Q5. Which one of the following best describes the
concept of ‘Small Farmer Large Field’?
(a) Resettlement of a large number of people
uprooted from their countries due to war, by giving
them a large cultivable land which they cultivate
collectively and share the produce
(b) Many marginal farmers in an area organize
themselves into groups and synchronize and
harmonize selected agricultural operations
(c) Many marginal farmers in an area together make a
contract with a corporate body and surrender their
land to the corporate body for a fixed term for which
the corporate body makes a payment of agreed
amount to the farmers
(d) A company extends loans, technical knowledge
and material inputs to a number of small farmers in an
area so that they produce the agricultural commodity
required by the company for its manufacturing
process and commercial production
The correct option is (b).
• Small Farmer Large Field (SFLF) is a collective
action model that helps small and marginal
farmers overcome diseconomies of scale and
low bargaining power.

•

•
•

Farmers organize into groups, synchronize
crop choice, and coordinate agricultural
operations from sowing to harvesting.
This increases productivity, reduces costs,
and enhances market bargaining power.
Hence option correct.

Q6. Consider the following statements:
1. The Government of India provides Minimum

How many of the above statements are correct?

The correct option is (c).

Government of India.
• Statement 2 is correct: It is grown as a Kharif
crop in India.
• Statement 3 is correct: Tribal populations in
several states use niger seed oil for cooking
and its press cake as livestock feed.
• Hence option correct.

Q8.Consider the following statements:
Statement-I: India accounts for 3.2% of global export
of goods.

Which one of the following is correct in respect of the
above statements?
(a) Both Statement-I and Statement-II are correct and

(b) Both Statement-I and Statement-II are correct and
Statement-II is not the correct explanation for
Statement-I
(c) Statement-I is correct but Statement-II is incorrect
(d) Statement-I is incorrect but Statement-II is correct
The correct option is (d).
• Statement I is incorrect: As per WTO data,
India’s share in global merchandise exports is
1.7%, not 3.2%.
• Statement II is correct: The PLI scheme
encourages both domestic and foreign
companies to set up or expand
manufacturing units in India, promoting
exports and job creation.

(d) Neither 1 nor 2

Q10.With reference to Central Bank Digital

Q11.In the context of finance, the term ‘beta’ refers
to:
(a) The process of simultaneous buying and selling of
an asset from different platforms
(b) An investment strategy of a portfolio manager to
balance risk versus reward
(c) A type of systemic risk that arises where perfect
hedging is not possible
(d) A numeric value that measures the fluctuations of
a stock to changes in the overall stock market
The correct option is (d).
• Beta measures the volatility (systematic risk)
of a stock relative to the overall market.
• Derived from the Capital Asset Pricing Model
(CAPM), it indicates how sensitive a stock’s
returns are to market movements.
o β = 1 → moves in line with market
o β > 1 → more volatile
o β < 1 → less volatile
o β < 0 → moves inversely
• Hence option correct.
Q12.In the context of Self-Help Groups (SHGs),
consider the following statements:
Statement-I: The Self-Help Group (SHG) programme
was initiated by NABARD and began extensively in
1991–92.
Statement-II: Regional Rural Banks and Scheduled
Commercial Banks support SHGs.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
• Statement I is correct: The SHG programme
was launched by NABARD in 1991–92,
marking the beginning of the SHG movement
in India.
• Statement II is correct: Regional Rural Banks
(RRBs) and Scheduled Commercial Banks

•

support SHGs by providing credit linkage and
financial inclusion services.
Hence option correct.

Q13.Consider the following statements:
Statement-I: India’s public sector health care system
largely focuses on curative care with limited
preventive, promotive and rehabilitative care.
Statement-II: Under India’s decentralized approach to
health care delivery, the States are primarily
responsible for organizing health services.
(a) Both Statement-I and Statement-II are correct and
Statement-II is the correct explanation for Statement-I
(b) Both Statement-I and Statement-II are correct and
Statement-II is not the correct explanation for
Statement-I
(c) Statement-I is correct but Statement-II is incorrect
(d) Statement-I is incorrect but Statement-II is correct
The correct option is (b).
• Statement I is correct: As per the National
Health Policy (2017), India’s public health
system emphasizes curative care, while
preventive and promotive services remain
limited.
• Statement II is correct: Under the
Constitution, health is a State Subject,
meaning States are responsible for organizing
and delivering health services.
• However, Statement II does not directly
explain Statement I.
• Hence option correct.
UPSC PYQs – 2024
Q1. Consider the following statements:
Statement-I: If the United States of America (USA)
were to default on its debt, holders of US Treasury
Bonds will not be able to exercise their claims to
receive payment.
Statement-II: The USA Government debt is not
backed by any hard assets, but only by the faith of the
Government.
Which one of the following is correct in respect of the
above statements?

(a) Both Statement-I and Statement-II are correct and
Statement-II explains Statement-I.
(b) Both Statement-I and Statement-II are correct, but
Statement-II does not explain Statement-I.
(c) Statement-I is correct, but Statement-II is
incorrect.
(d) Statement-I is incorrect, but Statement-II is
correct.
The correct option is (a).
• Statement I is incorrect: If the U.S.
government defaults on its debt payment,
American or foreign debt holders can litigate
or sue in either the U.S. District Court or the
U.S. Court of Federal Claims, which handles
disputes between the federal government and
U.S. contractors. Thus, holders of U.S.
Treasury Bonds can exercise their claims to
receive payment.
• Statement II is correct: U.S. Treasury
securities are backed by the full faith and
credit of the U.S. government. Investment
professionals use Treasury yields as the riskfree rate of return. The U.S. government has
never defaulted on an obligation, and it is
universally believed that it never will.
• Hence option correct.
Q2. Consider the following statements:
Statement-I: Syndicated lending spreads the risk of
borrower default across multiple lenders.
Statement-II: The syndicated loan can be a fixed
amount/lump sum of funds, but cannot be a credit
line.
Which one of the following is correct in respect of the
above statements?
(a) Both Statement-I and Statement-II are correct and
Statement-II explains Statement-I.
(b) Both Statement-I and Statement-II are correct, but
Statement-II does not explain Statement-I.
(c) Statement-I is correct, but Statement-II is
incorrect.
(d) Statement-I is incorrect, but Statement-II is
correct.
The correct option is (c).

•

•

•

Statement I is correct: A syndicated loan is
financing provided by a group of lenders
(syndicate) that work together to fund a
borrower. The borrower can be a corporation,
large project, or sovereign government. This
structure spreads the default risk among
multiple financial institutions.
Statement II is incorrect: A syndicated loan
can involve a fixed amount, a credit line, or a
combination of both. It is not limited to only a
lump sum.
Hence option correct.

Q3. Consider the following statements in respect of
the digital rupee:
1. It is a sovereign currency issued by the
Reserve Bank of India (RBI) in alignment with
its monetary policy.
2. It appears as a liability on the RBI’s balance
sheet.
3. It is insured against inflation by its very
design.
4. It is freely convertible against commercial
bank money and cash.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 1 and 3 only
(c) 2 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2 and 4
The correct option is (d).
• The Central Bank Digital Currency (CBDC) is
the RBI’s official form of currency,
interchangeable with the fiat rupee.
• Statement 1 is correct: CBDC is a sovereign
currency issued by the RBI in accordance with
its monetary policy.
• Statement 2 is correct: It appears as a liability
on the RBI’s balance sheet.
• Statement 3 is incorrect: The CBDC is not
insured against inflation by design.
• Statement 4 is correct: CBDC is freely
convertible into cash and commercial bank
money.
• Hence option correct.

and options

Q9.With reference to physical capital in Indian

Items

Category

Working capital

The correct option is (b).

in India.

Q11.With reference to Corporate Social
Responsibility (CSR) rules in India, consider the
following statements:
1. CSR rules specify that expenditures that

will not be considered as CSR activities.

on CSR activities.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct: CSR projects
benefiting only employees or the company
are not considered CSR.
o Examples: Marathons, sponsorships,
political donations, and compliance
expenses.

spend at least 2% of average net profits from
the previous 3 years on CSR activities.

Q12.With reference to the Indian economy,
“Collateral Borrowing and Lending Obligations” are
the instruments of:
(a) Bond market
(b) Forex market
(c) Money market
(d) Stock market
The correct option is (c).

•

•

•

•
•

Collateralised Borrowing and Lending
Obligation (CBLO) is a money market
instrument introduced by the Clearing
Corporation of India Ltd. (CCIL) in 2003.
It allows market participants to borrow and
lend funds against collateral in the short-term
money market.
Banks classify CBLO borrowings as “Liability
in India to Others,” qualifying for reserve
requirements.
RBI granted CRR exemption to promote CBLO
development.
Hence option correct.

Q1. With reference to investments, consider the

1. Bonds
2. Hedge Funds
3. Stocks
4. Venture Capital
How many of the above are treated as Alternative
Investment Funds?
(a) Only one
(b) Only two

(d) All the four
The correct option is (b).
• Alternative Investment Funds (AIFs) are
privately pooled investment vehicles that
collect funds from sophisticated investors to

invest in accordance with a defined
investment policy.
• They are distinct from traditional investments
like stocks and bonds.
• Hedge Funds and Venture Capital are classic
examples of AIFs.
o Hedge funds (Category III AIF) use
complex trading strategies.
o Venture Capital funds (Category I
AIF) invest in startups and early-stage
ventures.
• Bonds and Stocks are traditional investment
instruments and are not considered AIFs.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. Which of the following are the sources of income
for the Reserve Bank of India?
1. Buying and selling Government bonds
2. Buying and selling foreign currency
3. Pension fund management
4. Lending to private companies
5. Printing and distributing currency notes
(a) I, II and V
(b) I and III only
(c) II, IV and V
(d) All the above
The correct option is (a).
• The RBI has several sources of income:
o Open Market Operations: It earns
interest on government bonds it
holds and profits from favorable price
changes when buying and selling
them. (Statement I is correct)
o Foreign Exchange Operations: It
earns income by buying foreign
currency (like USD) at a lower price
and selling at a higher price.
(Statement II is correct)
o Seigniorage: It earns profit from
printing currency, as the face value
exceeds production cost. (Statement
V is correct)
• Pension fund management is handled by
EPFO and PFRDA, not the RBI. (Statement III
is incorrect)

•

RBI does not lend directly to private
companies, only to commercial banks.
(Statement IV is incorrect)
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. Consider the following statements:
1. The Reserve Bank of India mandates all listed
companies in India to submit a Business
Responsibility and Sustainability Report
(BRSR).
2. In India, a company submitting a BRSR makes
disclosures in the report that are largely nonfinancial in nature.
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) I only
(b) II only
(c) Both I and II
(d) Neither I nor II
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect: The mandate for
BRSR comes from the Securities and
Exchange Board of India (SEBI), not the RBI.
• Statement 2 is correct: The BRSR focuses on
Environmental, Social, and Governance (ESG)
parameters, which are largely non-financial
metrics designed to enhance investor
awareness and responsible business practices.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. Consider the following statements:
1. In India, income from allied agricultural
activities like poultry farming and wool rearing
in rural areas is exempted from any tax.
2. In India, rural agricultural land is not
considered a capital asset under the
provisions of the Income-tax Act, 1961.
Which one of the following is correct in respect of the
above statements?
(a) I only
(b) II only
(c) Both I and II
(d) Neither I nor II
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect: Income from
poultry, dairy farming, and bee hiving is not

considered agricultural income; it is taxable
as non-agricultural income.
• Statement 2 is correct: The Income-tax Act,
1961 explicitly excludes rural agricultural land
from the definition of a capital asset. Thus,
any gain from its sale is not subject to capital
gains tax.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. Consider the following statements:
1. India has joined the Minerals Security
Partnership as a member.
2. India is a resource-rich country in all the 30
critical minerals that it has identified.
3. The Parliament in 2023 amended the Mines
and Minerals (Development and Regulation)
Act, 1957 empowering the Central
Government to exclusively auction mining
leases for certain critical minerals.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) I and II only
(b) II and III only
(c) I and III only
(d) I, II and III
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct: India became the 14th
member of the Minerals Security Partnership
(MSP) in June 2023.
• Statement 2 is incorrect: India is not
resource-rich in all 30 identified critical
minerals; it imports key ones like lithium,
cobalt, and nickel.
• Statement 3 is correct: The MMDR
Amendment Act, 2023 empowered the
Central Government to exclusively auction
critical and strategic minerals.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6. Consider the following statements:
1. As regards returns from an investment in a
company, generally, bondholders are
considered to be relatively at lower risk than
stockholders.
2. Bondholders are lenders to a company
whereas stockholders are its owners.

3. For repayment purposes, bondholders are
prioritized over stockholders by a company.
Which one of the following is correct in respect of the
above statements?
(a) Both II and III explain I
(b) I and II only
(c) I and III only
(d) All three are unrelated
The correct option is (a).
• Bondholders are at lower risk than
stockholders.
• Statement 2: Bondholders are lenders who
receive fixed interest, while stockholders are
owners sharing in profits/losses.
• Statement 3: In case of liquidation,
bondholders have priority over stockholders
in repayment.
• Thus, both statements II and III explain why
bondholders face lower risk.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q7. Consider the following statements:
1. India accounts for a very large portion of all
equity option contracts traded globally.
2. India’s stock market has grown rapidly,
overtaking Hong Kong’s at one point.
3. There is no regulatory body to warn small
investors about options trading risks or act
against unregistered financial advisors.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) I and II only
(b) II and III only
(c) I only
(d) I, II and III
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct: India accounted for
over 84% of global equity option contracts
traded in early 2024.
• Statement 2 is correct: In January 2024,
India’s stock market surpassed Hong Kong,
becoming the world’s 4th largest.
• Statement 3 is incorrect: The SEBI regularly
warns investors and acts against unregistered
advisors.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q8. Consider the following statements:
1. Capital receipts create a liability or cause a
reduction in government assets.
2. Borrowings and disinvestment are capital
receipts.
3. Interest received on loans creates a liability of
the Government.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) I and II only
(b) II only
(c) I and III only
(d) All three
The correct option is (a).
• Capital receipts are receipts that create
liabilities (e.g., borrowings) or reduce assets
(e.g., disinvestment). (Statement I is correct)
• Borrowings and disinvestment meet this
definition. (Statement II is correct)
• Interest received on loans is non-tax
revenue, not a liability. (Statement III is
incorrect)
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q9. Suppose the revenue expenditure is ₹80,000
crore and the revenue receipts are ₹60,000 crore. The
government budget also shows borrowings of ₹10,000
crore and interest payments of ₹6,000 crore. Which of
the following are correct?
1. Revenue deficit is ₹20,000 crore.
2. Fiscal deficit is ₹10,000 crore.
3. Primary deficit is ₹4,000 crore.
(a) I only
(b) II only
(c) I and III only
(d) I, II and III
The correct option is (d).
• Revenue Deficit = Revenue Expenditure –
Revenue Receipts = 80,000 – 60,000 =
₹20,000 crore.
• Fiscal Deficit = Borrowings = ₹10,000 crore.
• Primary Deficit = Fiscal Deficit – Interest
Payments = 10,000 – 6,000 = ₹4,000 crore.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q10. A country’s fiscal deficit stands at ₹50,000 crore.
It is receiving ₹10,000 crore through non-debt capital
receipts. The country’s interest liabilities are ₹1,500
crore. What is the gross primary deficit?
(a) ₹48,500 crore
(b) ₹51,500 crore
(c) ₹58,500 crore
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (a).
• Gross Primary Deficit = Fiscal Deficit –
Interest Payments
• = ₹50,000 crore – ₹1,500 crore = ₹48,500
crore.
• Non-debt capital receipts do not affect this
calculation.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q11. Which of the following statements are correct
regarding the 15th Finance Commission?
1. It recommended grants of ₹4,800 crore for
2022–26 to enhance educational outcomes.
2. 45% of the net proceeds of Union taxes are to
be shared with States.
3. ₹45,000 crore are set as performance-based
incentives for agricultural reforms.
4. It reintroduced tax effort criteria to reward
fiscal performance.
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) I and II only
(b) I, II and IV
(c) I, III and IV
(d) II and III only
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct: ₹4,800 crore grant to
improve educational outcomes.
• Statement 2 is incorrect: The devolution
share remains at 41%, not 45%.
• Statement 3 is correct: ₹45,000 crore for
agriculture reform incentives.
• Statement 4 is correct: Tax effort criterion
reintroduced to reward efficiency in tax
collection.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q12. Consider the following statements regarding
IBRD:
1. It provides loans and guarantees to middleincome countries.
2. It works single-handedly to help developing
countries reduce poverty.
3. It was established to help Europe rebuild after
World War II.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) I only
(b) II only
(c) I and III only
(d) All three
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct: IBRD provides loans
and guarantees to middle-income and
creditworthy low-income countries.
• Statement 2 is incorrect: It works with other
World Bank Group institutions, not singlehandedly.
• Statement 3 is correct: Established in 1944
(Bretton Woods) to rebuild Europe post–
World War II.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q13. Consider the following statements:
1. In RTGS, the settlement time is instantaneous
while in NEFT, it takes some time.
2. In RTGS, the customer is charged for inward
transactions while NEFT is not.
3. RTGS operating hours are restricted while
NEFT is not.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) I only
(b) I and II only
(c) II and III only
(d) All three
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct: RTGS = Real-time
settlement; NEFT = batch settlement.
• Statement 2 is incorrect: Inward transactions
are free for both.
• Statement 3 is incorrect: Both RTGS and NEFT
are available 24×7×365 since Dec 2020.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q14. Consider the following countries:
1. United Arab Emirates
2. France
3. Germany
4. Singapore
5. Bangladesh
How many among the above accept international
merchant payments under UPI?
(a) Only two
(b) Only three
(c) Only four
(d) All five
The correct option is (c).
• Accepted: UAE , France , Singapore , (plus
Nepal among others).
• Not accepted: Germany , Bangladesh .
• Hence, four of the listed countries have UPI
international merchant acceptance.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q15. Consider the following activities:
1. Production of crude oil
2. Refining, storage and distribution of
petroleum
3. Marketing and sale of petroleum products
4. Production of natural gas
How many of the above activities are regulated by the
Petroleum and Natural Gas Regulatory Board in our
country?
(a) Only one
(b) Only two
(c) Only three
(d) All four
The correct option is (b).
• The PNGRB Act, 2006 mandates regulation of
refining, processing, storage, transportation,
distribution, marketing, and sale of

petroleum, petroleum products, and natural
gas.
• However, production of crude oil and natural
gas is explicitly excluded.
• Hence, activities II and III are regulated.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
